  Why the restart is unanswered: your first tab was never closed. Its own
  results at 9:20 still say "No folder picked yet", so it had not reloaded since
  before the pick, and Chrome was running throughout.

  What the run did show about folders, and it matters: the saved copy, a
  different file at a different path, opened with the folder already granted,
  without asking. To Chrome, every HTML file opened from disk is one site.

  - Anything we keep in the browser (a scan, a list of folders, a folder's
    handle) is readable by any other local HTML file opened in that profile.
  - A folder you open to us stands open to such a file for as long as the
    permission lives.
  - This is your "hostile HTML files flying around" concern from the other side:
    not their file being run by us, but what their file could reach of ours.
  - How long the permission lives is exactly what the restart shows, so the
    design for remembered folders waits on it. If Chrome asks again after a
    restart, "update all" costs one press and one prompt and nothing gets the
    folder silently. If it does not ask, keeping a folder's handle has to be
    your explicit choice.
  - It is also a reason for what we make to live in files you save, which the
    stored form is for, and not only in browser storage.

  One fault in my page: in the app window it reported "address bar true". The
  browser's own flag does not tell an app window from a tab; the page's judgment
  rested on the display mode, which was right.

  ### What keeping every file's text costs (whole DNN, 10,755 files kept, under
  Node)

  | | Reads | Peak memory | Time | Machine load |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | Before | 35,481 | 1,107 MB | 194 s | 44 |
  | After | 10,903 | 1,622 MB | 239 s | 20 |

  - Both scans wrote the same output (digest `2a4c87ca522483b6`).
  - So reading once costs about half again in memory at this size; on the
  Library alone the peak did not move. The two times are not comparable at those
  loads.
  - The page was **not** measured on whole DNN: the lane cost a 21-minute wait
  today.
  - 22 files are still read more than once under Node:
    - `.gitignore`, which discovery reads in each of its 3 walks
    - 21 `tsconfig.json` files that do not exist: each `package.json` reader
  looks for one in both passes, and failed reads are deliberately not kept.
  These are probes that step 4 ends.

  **What would release text, and why releasing at the last asker would not.**
  - The peak comes at the turn between the two passes, when every text is held.
  Each file's last asker is its own turn in the second pass, so releasing text
  after the last asker would not lower the peak.
  - What would lower it is a budget B: once B bytes are kept, keep no more. The
  second pass then finds the first B bytes' worth of files kept and reads the
  rest again: at most two reads where there were five, at a peak bounded by B.
  - Letting the oldest go first would find nothing, because both passes run in
  the same order.
  - This is in the design's section 8 as a fork; the owner's free memory sets B.

  ### Forks left to you
  - The read budget above.
  - The host's question about the folders above, as proposed.

  ## Second milestone: veil order and live drag are committed; the drag is
  quicker but does not yet "swing with the hand"; item 4 not started

  This took about 68 minutes, not fifty: the whole-suite run alone was 19
  minutes and the lane was contended (about 13 minutes waiting behind other
  builders).

  **Commit** on `worktree-agent-abfcd17682e3c7e13`, on top of `a959e1d20`:
  - `30afc2f67` Keep the main body's files in sight at the 3D start, and let the
  forces keep up with a dragged sphere

  No engine, generator, 2D or World Map file touched.

  ### 1. Veil order

  **Default is now (b): whole groups are veiled the smallest first; budget stays
  1,024.** Directory shells keep largest-first, and go before whole groups at
  the same distance. A veiled group's name now ends with its file count, as a
  shell's does.

  Matched pictures on this repository with nothing asked (132 spheres in the
  same places in all three):

  | | Rule | Files drawn in skins | Bodies veiled | Files hidden |
  | --- | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
  | a | as it was, largest first | 446 | 1 (main body) | 706 |
  | b | smallest whole groups first | 1,024 | 32 of 47 | 128 |
  | c | as b, budget 2,048 | 1,152 | 0 | 0 |

  Frame times while the camera is turned for three seconds (one run each):

  | | Card: median, 95th | Slowed (software WebGL, CPU 4x): frames, median, 95th
  |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | a | 47 ms, 54 ms | 13 frames, 305 ms, 668 ms |
  | b | 45 ms, 53 ms | 15 frames, 247 ms, 301 ms |
  | c | 30 ms, 44 ms | 15 frames, 246 ms, 327 ms |

  - **What the numbers say:** drawing 2.5 times as many files in skins made no
  frame slower on either machine. About four frames a second on the slowed
  machine whichever rule.
  - **My eye:** (a) is the complaint. (b) fixes the main body but 32 small
  groups are now pale balls, so the picture reads as a field of blanks; it hides
  fewer files and looks more hidden. (c) is the truest and best looking.
  - **Why (b) and not (c):** (b) is the rule the owner answered and needs no
  budget change. (c) is one number away, but this repository cannot draw more
  than 1,152 files in skins, so nothing here measures what 2,048 costs.

  **Side effect you should know:** the rule also applies with a pin. A pinned
  picture's far periphery now shows many small veiled islands, where before it
  veiled the few largest far bodies. I saw this in the drag pictures and did not
  compare it against the old rule.

  Pictures: `AI-Agent-Workspace/AgentSessionHistory/2026/11/2026-11-22/Screensho
  ts/veil-order/` (README, nine PNGs: a/b/c each at rest, turned once, turned
  twice; `measurements.txt`). The three variants were switched by a temporary
  hook, removed before the commit.

  ### 2. Drag

  **Cause found, two mends made, both in `motionForce`
  (`views/forceGraphLayout.ts`):**
  1. A dragged sphere moves at every frame, and a frame after such a move was
  held to one evaluation of the field. Under a moving hand that was every frame.
  Now only a sphere no hand holds counts.
  2. A frame took at most one tick. While a sphere is held, a frame now takes
  whole ticks while its 10 ms budget lasts, up to eight (`HAND_TICKS`, a
  starting value).

  Same drag of the pinned `graph.ts` on this repository (183 spheres, 12
  shells). "Came" is the median way at the hand's arrival, as a share of where
  each ends at rest:

  | Build | Frames during drag | Spheres | Shells | Rest after release |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | Before, card | median 88 ms, worst 247 | 30% | 60% | 16.5 s |
  | First mend, card | median 64 ms, worst 106 | 48% | 87% | 10.6 s |
  | First mend, slowed | median 292 ms, worst 513 | 49% | 85% | 53.7 s |

  - Rest after release is the same within two units (median way 102.9, 103.0,
  100.8).
  - **My eye:** the before and after pictures differ less than the numbers.
  After, the shells have come with the hand but there is still a fan of

  - **What follows:** tied files and their directory shells. Every other sphere

  - **Not looked at:** a wrapped group or a veiled body under the hand; the

  - Settle Hold untouched.

› Message from general-purpose
  ## Handback: an author's documentation reference is drawn, as the author's
  word (the second form)

  **Commit** (worktree branch, on top of `056f70c7d`):
  - `7fcc13c55` Draw an author's documentation reference as a relation of its
  own, apart from the edges. Reader, composer, grammar, graph, oracle
  comparison, tests, regenerated docs, the format and adapter documents, and the
  record.

  Scratch folder removed. The history was not rebuilt.

  ### How a reference is resolved
  It goes through the C# reader's existing name resolution and has no resolver
  of its own.
  - `resolveChain` is now a thin wrapper over a new `resolvePrefix`, which also
  returns the segments after the type. It uses the same containers, `using`s,
  aliases, project reach and per-segment arity as code.
  - The scope is the declaration being documented. For a type's own comment that
  scope is inside the type.
  - `crefName` in `csharp.xmldoc.ts` only parses the syntax: segments with their
  arity, plus the parameter list.

  Where each kind of reference lands:
  - **A type:** every part that declares it.
  - **`Type.Member`:** the part that declares the member, looking in the type's
  parts and then in the types it inherits from.
  - **Overloads:** chosen by parameter count and by each type's simple name
  (`int` is read as `Int32`). If none matches that way, all overloads with that
  count; if none has that count, none.
  - **Constructors:** written as `#ctor`, or as the type's own name with a
  parameter list.
  - **A name alone:** finds a member that the documented type, or a type
  enclosing it, declares itself. It does not find one the type only inherits.
  That rule comes from the compiler's answers on DNN, not from assumption:
    - `<see cref="Settings"/>` in a class `Settings` that inherits a property
  `Settings` means the class.
    - `<see cref="Layout"/>` in an appender that inherits a property `Layout`
  means the namespace `log4net.Layout`.

  Where the reader's facts cannot tell:
  - A member with no symbol (not public, or an enum member, which the reader now
  holds but does not show) lands on its type's anchor in the right file.
  - Overloads whose parameter types share a simple name are not told apart, and
  both are linked.
  - Operators, indexers and conversions resolve to nothing.
  - A documentation ID such as `M:…` is kept as written and not resolved. The
  compiler copies it without resolving it: DNN has 405 of them and the
  compiler's record has none.

  ### What the Live Docs and the graph carry
  - **Live Doc:** a resolved reference reads `-
  [`Base.Gadget`](./Base.cs.mdmd.md#symbol-gadget)`.
    - A reference that names several declarations gets one line per declaration.
    - An unresolved one stays plain code.
    - References on `exception`, `inheritdoc`, `permission` and any other
  element join the list with the element's word, e.g. `` `Lib.Thing` (exception)
  ``.
    - The anchor is the symbol at the declaration's line, so an overload lands
  on its own heading. The workspace index now carries each symbol's line.
    - `documentationReferenceLine` and `readDocumentationReference` in
  `document.ts` are the only places that know this line's form.
  - **Graph:** each file gets `documentation?: DocumentationEdge[]` (from the
  symbol's slug, to a file and slug, with the element). Each target gets
  `documentedBy?: string[]`.
    - Both are present only when non-empty.
    - Neither is part of `edges`, `outbound` or `inbound`.
    - `estateGraph` prefixes their paths when it merges scans.

  **Rendering faults mended, each seen failing first:**
  - A generic was written as its plain namesake (`Lib.Helper{T}` as
  `Lib.Helper`), because its angle brackets were stripped as a tag.
  - A `<seealso cref/>` inside a summary vanished and left "; ".

  **Also mended, because the compiler measurement showed them:**
  - A documentation comment is now taken across an ordinary comment or a
  `#pragma`, as the compiler takes it.
  - A `<see>` with neither attribute, and a relative `href`, are no longer read
  as a `cref`. A relative href is now a link of no kind, written without
  backticks.

  ### Results against the compiler
  **Samples:** on all five, the reader's pairs are exactly the compiler's.
  `Documented.cs` has 6, and all 13 of its references are answered as the
  compiler answers them. The estate sample has 1; the other three have none. The
  `DOCUMENTATION` register gained `related` and `differences` for every sample,
  and every sample's `differences` is empty.

  **Corpora:** I wrote the compiler records again into my scratch copies,
  because the kept records from 2026-11-21 had no `cref`. The code edges and the
  compiler's pair counts came out as before.

  | | DNN | mojoPortal |
  |---|---:|---:|
  | pairs the compiler names | 1,312 | 4 |
  | pairs the reader's relation makes | 1,313 | 4 |
  | of them the compiler's | 1,259 | 4 |
  | of the 294 only the author's word makes | 283 | (none such) |
  | the compiler's only | 53 | 0 |
  | the reader's only | 54 | 0 |

  **DNN differences by cause.** Every pair is listed in
  `Probes/authors-word/relation-dnn.txt`; the mojoPortal run is in
  `relation-mojoportal.txt`.

  The compiler's only (53):
  - **38 — the reader keeps no documentation where the reference stands,**
  because the declaration has no symbol.
    - Enum member: `FolderController.cs` `StorageLocationTypes.*` →
  `StandardFolderProvider.cs`.
    - Private member: `RssHandler.cs`'s private `GetRssItem` →
  `GenericRssElement.cs`; `ForwardingAppender.cs`'s private field →
  `IAppenderAttachable.cs`.
  - **15 — the reader reads the reference and resolves it to nothing.**
    - 11 are members of log4net's `LoggingEvent`, whose base list holds an `#if
  … #endif` that the syntax tree cannot read whole (`LoggingEvent.Domain`,
  `LoggingEvent.Level`).
    - 3 are `PropertiesDictionary`, declared once in each `#if`/`#else` branch,
  so the every-branch reading sees two declarations and calls it ambiguous.
    - 1 is `Mode`, brought in by `using static`, which the reader does not read
  in code either.

  The reader's only (54):
  - **32 — in an `#if` section the compiler's fixed preprocessor symbols leave
  inactive.** All are in log4net, whose project defines `NET_2_0;NET_4_0`.
  Examples: `AspNetTraceAppender.cs` → `AppenderSkeleton.cs`,
  `ILogExtensions.cs` → `ILog.cs`.
  - **15 — the target's project is reached only through another project.** The
  compiler program references direct project references only. Example:
  `TelerikUtils.cs` and `DeleteFilesStep.cs` → `IApplicationStatusInfo.cs`. The
  same pairs show up as adapter-only code edges.
  - **4 — a parameter type the oracle cannot bind,** because it has no framework
  references: `IEventLogger.AddLog(string,…)`,
  `Crypto.Encrypt(System.IO.Stream,string)`.
  - **2 — the compiler resolves to nothing:** `DnnLogger.cs`
  (`log4net.ObjectRenderer.IObjectRenderer`) and `IFlushable.cs`
  (`RemotingAppender`, declared inside an inactive section).
  - **1 — the compiler resolves to another declaration:**
  `ILoggerRepository.cs`'s `Hierarchy` is the namespace, which C# finds before
  the type a `using` brings in. The reader looks for types only, in code as
  here.

› Message from general-purpose
  Two blind readers were written from the specification alone, one in Python and
  one in Go. On every sample, both agree with the module and with each other,
  except one valid kind of file that the module refuses. It refuses it by a
  limit the specification never states. The records are committed on
  `worktree-agent-aff87bc5aa55996cb`, on top of `6c1a34554`: `d7b7ec38f` holds
  the Python reader and `5a8b61c5b` the Go reader. The record is
  `AI-Agent-Workspace/AgentSessionHistory/2026/11/2026-11-22/Probes/stored-form-
  second-reader/README.md`. The module, its tests and the specification are
  untouched.

  **What each blind run could see**
  - **How it was run:** a headless `claude-opus-5-5` at high effort. Its only
  tool was Bash, with no MCP server, no settings except a sandbox, and a
  one-line system prompt.
  - **The sandbox:** every command ran in Claude Code's own sandbox, which could
  write only in its folder and had no network. It could not read `/workspaces`,
  `/home`, `/root`, `/tmp`, `/vscode`, `/go` or `/opt`.
  - **Its folder** was in `/var/tmp` and held six files:
    - the specification with only its two top lines removed;
    - the estate's stored form and text form;
    - the empty scan and `everyFact`, the package's two smallest made scans, in
  both forms.
  - **The blindness check,** before each run, confirms all of that, quoted in
  the record:
    - `/workspaces`, `/tmp` and the home folder were empty or missing;
    - network requests got 403;
    - writing outside the folder was read-only;
    - it had one tool;
    - what still reached it was the account email, the tool's and sandbox's own
  descriptions, and the environment lines.
  - **Two leaks were found and closed:**
    - Claude Code's sandbox temporary folder defaults to `/tmp/claude-1000`,
  which holds every session's scratch. A Haiku trial found it readable, so each
  run now gets its own `TMPDIR`.
    - The Go run was first started while the Python run's folder was still in
  `/var/tmp`. I stopped it within seconds, before its sandbox was set up, moved
  the folder away, and restarted it after its own check. The harness now refuses
  to start while anything else is in `/var/tmp`.

  **Samples, all made by the module, with its answer for each in
  `module-answers.json`**
  - **Valid:**
    - this repository's scan and DNN's (10,777 files, 36 MB of text), both ways;
    - six seeded made scans;
    - four of my own: the ordering hard case (U+E000, U+FFFD, U+10000, U+1F600
  linking to each other), every character the text rules name, numbers at 0 and
  2^32−1, and every enumeration word (including `Constraints`, `opening`,
  `declared`).
  - **To be refused:**
    - 400 fuzzer mutants (seeds 0–399, all eight kinds, three bases); the module
  accepts 5, frame swaps with itself;
    - the 9 not-canonical files from the unit cases;
    - 15 of my own one-change files with every hash recomputed;
    - 23 rewritten texts.

  **How the blind readers answered**
  - **Python:** DNN's text matches byte for byte, and its content name matches
  when computed from the text. Every corpus and made scan matches. All 395
  mutants, 9 unit files, 12 probe files and 23 texts are refused as the module
  refuses them.
  - **Each other's tests:** the module agrees with all 157 refusal cases the
  Python reader wrote for itself, and all 161 the Go reader wrote. The two
  readers give identical answers on both sets.
  - **Go:** identical results to Python on everything.

  **Disagreements: one, the same file shape seen three ways**
  - **Where it shows:** my `probe-text-past-the-file-bound.fpk`, and the Python
  reader's own `text-expansion-300MB.fpk`. Each is a valid file of about 7 KB
  naming one long string many times.
  - **What happens:** the module's `textOf` refuses it ("the text would hold …
  bytes of names … past" 64 × file + 1 MiB). Both blind readers print the text,
  65.7 MB and 300.9 MB. Their bytes match each other and the module's own
  `textOfScan`.
  - **Which kind of finding:** against the module, since section 6 calls such a
  file canonical and no section bounds the text. The text is also silent. The
  module's rule is the safer one, so the fix belongs in the specification: state
  the export's bound in section 8 or 3.5.
  - No disagreement was a blind reader's fault, so no round went back to either
  run.

  **Findings against the specification, from the 14 Python and 20 Go questions**
  (all listed in the record and committed in each `questions.md`)
  - **4.5:** the wires grammar writes a `first` even when the count is 0. The
  module and the sample write none.
  - **Rules the text never states,** where the module and both readers happen to
  agree:
    - no trailing bytes in the head, strings, paths or wires (4.1–4.5);
    - a text's facts must pass the binary layout's checks (8);
    - the order of each file's lines, and that `authored` is required (8);
    - which object members are always present (8, 4.3);
    - the enumeration words used in the text, such as `Constraints` (8, 4.3);
    - which links make wires: section 4.5 says "links", but only section 5's
  edges work (4.5, 5);
    - what `extension` may hold (4.1);
    - "declared length of the strings sections" (4.4), which has two readings;
  neither changes what is accepted.
  - **3.4 / 3.5:** whether a set of facts can be stored depends on the
  compressor, because the inflation limit is reckoned against the compressed
  length.
  - **3.4 against section 6, step 4:** check the name from the headers alone, or
  inflate the end frame. Both can hold, but the text does not say which check
  is meant.
  - **3.2:** which raw deflate streams are valid in RFC 1951's corner cases.
  zlib and Go's `compress/flate` may decide differently. This is not measured.
  - **2.4:** the drive rule refuses ordinary Unix names such as `a:b.txt`. This
  is a cost of the rule, for the owner to weigh.
  - **Small:** the text names code a second reader cannot see (`comparePaths`,
  `graphOf` and others).
  - **Not the specification's:** the exit status of a refusal was left open by
  my brief. The comparison counts a single `refused:` line as a refusal whatever
  the status.

  **Time and memory limits** (10 s plus 60 s per MiB, and address space of 1 GiB
  plus 256 bytes per input byte)
  - **Python** never ran past either limit; the most it used of a time limit was
  9%.
    - DNN's stored form: 11.3 s, 63 MB peak. DNN's text: 12.5 s, 463 MB peak.
    - The 50 bombs: each refused within 0.4 s, at most 22 MB.
  - **Go** stopped once, on `made-seeded-11-773.txt` in `name` mode. It died
  inside the Go runtime's startup (`runtime.schedinit`) before reading a byte,
  under exactly 1,170 MiB of address space.
    - Rerun alone at 1,024, 1,100, 1,300 and 2,048 MiB, it gave the module's
  name every time, and its unlimited peak is 12 MB.
    - It fails again at exactly 1,170 MiB. That is Go's reservation behaviour
  under `prlimit --as`, not memory the reader used, and the record says so.
    - Under a machine load of 36 to 50, DNN read in 2.2 s and its text named in
  8.2 s (210 MB peak).

  **Cost:** $7.30 in all. Python implementer $2.64 (876 s), Go implementer $4.29
  (1,400 s), blindness checks $0.18 and $0.11, Haiku sandbox trials about

  the picture and the board agree. Graph-drawing designers call this keeping
  the mental map: things stay where you last saw them.

    the two apart. In a mirrored picture, only colour and the key say it.

    round. That has to be shown as a turn, or it will feel like the map jumped.

  unchanged, built once the current way-down piece lands. It is built as one
  property of a picture (which way it reads), because a right-to-left layout

  ## Fourth milestone: `inspect --from <file>` now prints an author's
  documentation reference, and the terminal-path walk is deleted

  I merged `101d57def` first, plain and alone; it fast-forwarded. One commit,
  `fe497f3fe`, holds both items. Item 3 (a directory as `--from` and `--to`)
  still waits for the two Explorer branches, as you said.

  ### 1. An author's documentation reference
  After a file's neighbours, `inspect --from <file>` prints two lists, each
  under a heading of its own:
  - **What its documentation names.** The heading is `<file>'s documentation
  names <n> declaration(s) in <m> file(s) (the author's word, not a use).`, then
  one line a reference, as `  <label>[ (<element>)] in <file>`.
  - **Whose documentation names it.** The heading is `<file> is named by the
  documentation of <n> file(s) (the author's word, not a use).`, then the files.

  Each list is printed only when it has something in it, and is cut at 200 with
  `… and N more, not listed`, as the neighbours list is.

  `--json` adds `documentation: { names: { count, references: [{ label,
  element?, to }], cut }, namedBy: { count, files, cut } }` to the `neighbours`
  payload.

  `--reachable`, paths and the unreachable list are unchanged. Nothing new feeds
  them: the relation is read only by the emitter.

  On the names sample:
  ```
  App/Documented.cs uses 0 files directly (outbound).
  App/Documented.cs is used by 0 files directly (inbound).
  App/Documented.cs's documentation names 11 declarations in 6 files (the
  author's word, not a use).
    Base in App/Base.cs
    …
    Derived (permission) in App/Derived.cs
    …
    Base.Gadget (inheritdoc) in App/Base.cs
  ```
  `App/HelperOfT.cs` prints `… is named by the documentation of 1 file (the
  author's word, not a use).` followed by `  App/Documented.cs`.

  **Cases**, in `tests/integration/live-docs/cli-answers.test.ts`, on a copy of
  the names sample:
  - the relation printed both ways, with the element's word, and in JSON;
  - `Documented.cs` has no neighbour either way, reaches nothing either way, and
  no route runs to any of the six files it names (`No path runs between …,
  either way.`).

  **Seen failing once:** with the documentation targets wired into the
  neighbours lists, both cases failed. The second failed on its neighbours
  count, which it checks first. With the wiring taken out, both pass.

  **One existing case moved:** `IPaymentService.cs`'s direct-users case now also
  expects `is named by the documentation of 1 file … Hub/PaymentHub.cs`,
  because today's merge linked PaymentHub's reference.

  **The ledger** gains a row in a new section, "Only the command line, for now",
  with a new status, "the command line's own". The printer counts such rows
  apart, and the test holds them as equivalents. The parity test now also
  generates the names sample. The table's totals: 32 rows; 20 with an equivalent
  (4 of them only as files or JSON); 11 gaps (7 with no command, 4 where a
  command lacks part); and 1 the command line's own, which the page does not
  draw yet. The CLI reference, the skill's `reference.md` and the parity

  ### 2. `enumerateTerminalPaths` deleted

› Message from general-purpose
  ## Stored form, third milestone: every finding answered in the spec, the
  documentation relation carried, all checks green; lazy graph and browser tab
  not done

  **Commit:** `7ba7acb27` "Answer the blind readers' findings in the stored
  form's specification, and carry the documentation relation". It is on
  `worktree-agent-a5522d16dedb937a3`, on top of `201fdfcc7`. The merge of
  `201fdfcc7` was a plain fast-forward. The record is appended under "Third
  milestone" in `Probes/stored-form-module/README.md`; I did not edit the second
  reader's record.

  **What held:** `npm run lint`, `npm run build`, the three audits,
  `docs:links:enforce`, the package's cases and fuzzer, and the whole unit
  project run once (3,670 of 3,670 cases in 241 files). Live Docs are
  regenerated, and the new test file has an authored Purpose and Notes.

  ### Findings, in the record's order
  1. **Text export bound (§3.5):** the spec now states the module's rule.
     - A reader may decline to write the text when the names it would write
  (UTF-8 bytes, each string counted once for every time a record names it)
  exceed `64 × file + 1 MiB`.
     - It must say it is declining the text, not the file. The file stays valid:
  it can be read, answered from, given its graph and its name.
     - Writing the text anyway is also correct. The spec requires the text for
  files within the bound and allows either answer past it, so both blind readers
  and the module are right.
     - The module did not change for this.
  2. **Wires grammar:** now `n:count [ first gapMinusOne{n−1} ]`, the bracket
  present only when n ≥ 1. A file with no wires is the single byte `00`.
  3. **Rules the module enforced but the text never stated, now all stated:**
     - no byte after the last item, as one general rule for all six sections
  (§3.2);
     - what makes a text valid beyond its form (§8): every check on the facts
  applies, so a valid text is exactly the text some valid file gives. The
  inflation bound is excluded, since it depends on the writer;
     - the one order of a file's lines, and which lines are required;
     - every always-present member, and every member present by a mark;
     - the enumeration words, kept apart from the graph's edge kinds, and the
  field-name map between the two grammars;
     - which links make wires: only dependencies' and types' targets, by §5's
  rule, said in §4.5;
     - the "declared length" of the strings sections is the sum of the strings
  frames' `rawLength`.
     - **`extension`, decided:** it may hold no `/`, `\` or NUL, and may be
  empty. I found no reason to allow a backslash or a NUL. **The module
  changed:** the writer and the reader both refuse them. The case failed first,
  then passed.
  4. **Storability and the compressor:** the bound stays reckoned from the
  compressed file, because that is what keeps a reader's cost bounded by the
  file it is handed. The spec now says plainly that near the bound, whether a
  set of facts can be stored depends on the writer, while no reader's verdict on
  a given file does.
  5. **Code the text names:** every mention of the module's code, its tests, the
  engine, `JSON.stringify`, `CompressionStream` or a platform version is now a
  sentence marked **Provenance**. A line at the top says no rule rests on one.

  **The Go reader's further questions:**
  - **The end (§3.4):** a reader must inflate the end, check it against its
  hash, and compare it with the name. It may first check from the headers alone
  that the end's header hash is SHA-256 of the name. That header check never
  replaces inflating, since it does not check the end's stored bytes. Both hold
  for a file a correct writer wrote.
  - **Raw deflate corner cases (§3.2):** the spec names them: incomplete or
  over-subscribed Huffman codes, distance codes 30 and 31, length codes 286 and
  287, and bits after the final block.
    - It states the guarantee: every section's content is hashed, so two correct
  readers can never be shown two maps from one file, only a map and a refusal.
    - Whether zlib and Go's inflater split on any of these is **unmeasured**;
  such streams were not cheap enough to build this milestone.
    - One data point: a stream with its last byte's high bit set was accepted by
  Node's zlib with the same output. Whether that bit was padding was not
  established.
  - **Drive rule's cost (§2.4):** `a:b.txt` at the scanned folder's top, and
  anything under a top folder named `c:…`, cannot be stored. The scanner leaves
  such a file out and lists it with its reason. Only a path's first two
  characters are tested, so `src/a:b.txt` is stored.
  - Also now stated: sids are first met in the layout's record order, not the
  text's line order; `[array]` and `[promise]` are marks that carry no bytes;
  the file length in the bound is the whole input, signature included.

  **The blind readers' refusal cases:**
  `packages/stored-form/test/storedForm.blind.test.ts` holds 25 cases, each
  named after the blind reader's file. They cover trailing bytes in head,
  strings, paths and wires, `wires-n0-with-first`, a wrong end count and a raw
  length longer than its stream, and six kinds of misordered or doubled text
  lines, plus others the package did not already make. The module refused all of
  them before they arrived, so none could be seen failing.

  ### The documentation relation
  - **The fork:** the engine reads the references from `Links` lines written in
  its own markdown grammar. Re-implementing that grammar in the module would be
  a second copy of it. Instead each symbol now carries `documentation`: the
  scanner's reading of each reference (name, element, link, and the target and
  anchor it names), kept beside the verbatim lines. This is ruling 3's
  principle: carried as the scanner read it, not checked, and said so.
  - **The rule is the text's own (§5):** an item for each reference whose target
  is a scanned file; `documentedBy` is the reverse, never the file itself; and
  the relation is never an edge or a wire.
  - **Layout and text:** the layout gains `list(docReference)` per symbol
  (§4.3), and the text form gains a `documentation` member on every symbol. The
  version stays 1, since nothing outside this work has written the format.
  - **Probe:** `scan-of-graph.ts` builds the reading with the engine's own
  `readDocumentationReference` and `linkTarget`, as the sink would.
  - **Red first:** the `graphOf` and text-form case failed with "expected
  undefined to deeply equal …", then passed.

  **Measured again on the three corpora, regenerated by the tip's generator:**

  | Corpus | Files | Documentation references | `documentedBy` entries | Stored
  vs `index.json` gzipped |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | This repository | 1,325 | 17 in 3 files | 8 | 1.11 MB vs 1.41 MB |
  | Estate | 41 | 1 | 1 | 7.8 KB vs 10.5 KB |
  | DNN | 10,777 | 5,637 in 1,586 files | 1,313 | 2.24 MB vs 3.79 MB |

  On all three, the graph read back still prints byte for byte as each
  `index.json`, and both round trips (binary → text → binary and text → binary →
  text) are exact.

  ### Unfinished
  - **The graph built only when a consumer asks for it:** not built. It is the
  last 15–20 % of the whole read with the graph.
  - **The browser tab through the lane:** not run.

  ### Harness
  It refused one command, a heredoc that appended a test case and ran an inline
  Python edit, as "too complex to verify"; it did not name git. I made the same
  edits with the file tools. The scratch folder is removed.

› Message from general-purpose
  ## Milestone 3: two commits, at 50 minutes; the reader probes not yet moved

  Branch `worktree-agent-ac72d8a99b54c2e5d`. I merged `4182a2693` (a
  fast-forward), then added:
  - `610af3c6e`: take a package's module entry from `exports`, as Node does, and
  from `main` only when there is no `exports`.
  - `3a7fff082`: bound what a scan keeps of its files by a read budget, and copy
  each file's first bytes instead of keeping a view of the whole file.

  ### The `exports` mend (its own commit)
  - **Seen red first:** with `packages/stored-form/src/storedForm.ts` added to
  `expected/repository-entries.json` as "module of
  packages/stored-form/package.json", the entries test failed.
  - **Node's own resolver agrees:** `require.resolve` and `import.meta.resolve`
  of `@live-documentation/stored-form` both give `dist/storedForm.js`, which the
  tsconfig maps back to `src/storedForm.ts`.
  - **What the reader now does (`packageModules` in `json.ts`):** when a
  manifest has `exports`, it resolves the subpath `.` and does not read `main`,
  as Node does not. `.` may be a string, a list of fall-backs, or conditions,
  written under `.` or as the whole of `exports`. A `null` means not exported.
  Without `exports`, it reads `main`.
  - **Conditions read:** those Node matches by default, `node`, `node-addons`,
  `import` or `require`, `module-sync` and `default`, once for a program that
  imports the package and once for one that requires it. That gives one or two
  files.
  - **Conditions not read:** `types`, because it names declarations, not code;
  and a bundler's own conditions such as `browser`, which Node never matches.
  - **A manifest whose `exports` holds only patterns** (such as `./*`, as the
  engine's and the Explorer's do) has no module entry, as before.
  - **Tests:** a new test, `json.exports.test.ts`, installs eleven manifest
  shapes as packages under `node_modules` and holds the reader to Node's
  resolver, asked both ways. Seven shapes failed with the old reader; all pass
  now. The entries test passes.
  - **Docs that changed:** the JSON reader's, the new test's,
  `packages/stored-form/src/storedForm.ts`'s (it gains its Entry line), and the
  expected entries'.

  ### The read budget, and what building it found
  - **Built as you ruled:** `workspaceReads(host, keptHead, budget)`, exposed as
  the generator's `readBudget` option with a default `READ_BUDGET`.
    - Until the budget is spent, every file read whole is kept for the run.
    - After it, a file is held only until the next one is read, so the asks
  within one file's turn still share one read.
    - The generator now asks for a file's reading before its first bytes, so a
  file the budget did not keep is read once in its turn, not twice.
    - Unit cases cover the budget; they were written before any whole-DNN number
  came in.
  - **The finding:** most of the morning's +515 MB was a mistake of mine in step
  3, not the kept text. I cut each file's first bytes (kept for the binary
  test) with `slice`, which on Node's `Buffer` is a view of the same memory, so
  each kept head held its whole file.
    - The clue: a 64 MiB budget, which DNN's texts never fill, changed neither
  the reads nor the peak.
    - Mended to copy (`new Uint8Array(bytes.subarray(…))`). A unit case fails on
  the view; I watched it fail.

  **Whole DNN under Node, mended code** (every read already in the disk's cache;
  each run from a frozen copy of its engine; same output digest
  `2a4c87ca522483b6` in all four):

  | Run | Machine load | Time | Peak memory | Reads |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | Before step 3 | 10.5 | 276.9 s | 1,116 MB | 35,481 |
  | Keep everything | 26.1 | 196.5 s | 1,381 MB | 10,903 |
  | 16 MiB | 16.7 | 155.6 s | 1,203 MB | 16,692 |
  | Keep nothing | 11.4 | 110.1 s | 1,399 MB | 21,896 |

  - **Fork decided, against the letter of your ruling:** the default stays at
  "keep everything". Time followed the machine's load, not the budget, and the
  peaks are within one another's noise. Re-reading costs nothing with a warm
  disk cache, so this measurement cannot choose. The default should be chosen in
  a page on the slowed machine, where every read waits on the browser; that run
  was not made. If you would rather take the ruling literally, the smallest
  budget within noise here is zero.
  - **Past the budget, a C# file is read three times, not two:** twice in the
  first pass (once for the table of types, which is built from every file before
  the file's own turn, then once for its own reading), and once in the second
  pass. The design said "at most two"; corrected.
  - **The design now carries the paragraph you asked for** on what would remove
  the budget: a reader that parses a file once and yields both what the index
  needs and what its own doc needs, binding against the index afterwards without
  the text. Not built.

  ### Ruling 4: this repository, `node_modules` and all
  I ran the probe count in place over this worktree, as a dry run with its own
  configuration (`probes-in-place.mts`).

  | Probes | Count |
  |---|---|
  | All | 15,452 |
  | Of a kept file | 2,866 |
  | Of a folder of kept files | 2,238 |
  | Of a path absent on disk | 8,504 |
  | Of the docs root | 14 |
  | Outside the workspace | 10 |
  | On disk but outside the listing | 1,820 |

  - **All 1,820 are discovery's** `gitIgnored.ts` looking for `.git`. No
  reader's probe, including `dependencies.ts`'s TypeScript resolution, reached
  `node_modules` or any other file outside the listing.
  - **Prediction:** moving the probes changes nothing here. The whole-docs
  comparison before and after, which your ruling requires, still has to be made
  when the probes actually move.

  ### Rulings 2 and 3

  ## Third milestone: budget raised, a veil order that keeps both pictures, and

  ### 1. The budget

  - **`DRAWN_FILES` is 2,048, one number for a body and for a picture.** This
  repository veils nothing at it, with or without a pin.
  - **Where the budget buys frames is not answered. 2,048 stays.**

  How far the DNN measurement got:
  - The corpus was copied to scratch and built as a sample in 2 min 11 s: 10,776
  files, 25,265 edges.
  - Only budgets 1,024 and 2,048 on the card were run.

  | Budget | Rest | Files drawn in skins | Bodies veiled | Frames while turning
  |
  | --- | --- | ---: | ---: | --- |
  | 1,024 | not within 81 s | 1,001 | 48 | 33 in 3 s, median 91 ms |
  | 2,048 | at 80 s | 1,446 | 1 | 50 in 3 s, median 63 ms |

  - These two frame times do not compare the budgets: the first picture was
  still moving when the camera turned.
  - 4,096 and none on the card were queued behind two other builders' holds, and
  I gave them up for time.

  card, so a slowed run would not fit a five-minute hold.

  **What the DNN picture does show:** it is 3,313 spheres (58 groups, 3,255 lone
  files), and at 2,048 the one veiled body is the main body, a blank ball at
  the centre. It is veiled because it alone holds more files than the budget. No
  veiling order can keep it; only a larger budget or drawing a body in part
  can. The owner's complaint returns at this size.

  `measurements.txt`). The sample is still at `dist/explorer/samples/dnn` in my

  ### 2. Smallest-first with a pin

  Matched pictures of `graph.ts` pinned, at rest, 61 bodies:

  | Picture | Budget | Files drawn | Bodies veiled |
  | --- | ---: | ---: | --- |

  ### 3. Drag trial

  - **Lever:** Tuning, 3D Layout, Forces, **Drag**: Holds its ground (default,
  unchanged) or Swings. It travels in a link when not default.
  - **Swings:** while a sphere is held, everything tied to it by a pulling wire
  or a chain of them has its anchor brought to where it stands at each call, so
  the tether pulls it nowhere. On release the picture rests where it lies.
  - **Why "rests where it lies" and no third value:** today's release already
  holds the picture from where it lies. A group that slid back after being
  carried would be a rubber band; I did not build it and did not look at it.

  One run each on the card, same drag of pinned `graph.ts` (shares are of the
  hand's way):

  | | Holds its ground | Swings |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | 49 tied files at the hand's arrival | 1.09 | 1.07 |
  | 12 shells at arrival | 0.75 | 0.87 |
  | 144 others at arrival | 0.23 | 0.21 |
  | Shells at rest after release | 0.78 | 0.87 |
  | Rest after release | 5.6 s | 5.2 s |

  - **My eye:** holding, the pinned file arrives with its tied files in a knot
  around it and a long fan of wires back to the group's two big shells.
  Swinging, the shells are beside the hand, the fan is short, and the group
  reads as one thing that came along. The pictures differ more than the numbers.
  - **Correction to my second handback:** I said tied files end at half the

  ## Stored form, fourth milestone: all four tasks done; your hypothesis on the
  doubled fact measured wrong, so both forms are kept

  **Commit:** `3508a1895` "Measure the stored form's documentation held twice,
  prove each blind case by its own check, and build the graph as asked". It is
  on `worktree-agent-a5522d16dedb937a3`, on top of `6da0f7817`, which I merged
  in plain and alone first. The record is under "Fourth milestone" in
  `Probes/stored-form-module/README.md`.

  New probe files beside it:
  - `links-lines.ts`: the doubled-fact measure.
  - `check-removal.mjs`, with its results in `check-removal.json`: takes each
  check out once.
  - `tab.mjs`: builds the tab's page and drives it.
  - `read-time.mjs` gained a `view` mode.

  **What held:** `npm run lint`, `npm run build`, the three audits,
  `docs:links:enforce`, `live-docs:generate` and its `--check` (exit 0, all
  files unchanged), the package's 48 cases with the fuzzer, and the whole unit
  project run once (3,679 of 3,679 cases in 242 files). No command was refused
  by the harness.

  ### 1. The fact held twice: measured, and your hypothesis is wrong
  `links-lines.ts` asks, using the engine's own reader and composer, whether
  `documentationReferenceLine(readDocumentationReference(line))` gives back each
  line of every `Links` section:

  | Corpus | Lines | Composer's line for a (name, element, link) reading | Not |
  Reference lines with the author's words |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | This repository | 43 | 42 | 1: a plain path, `- ../../docs/registry.md —
  Widget architecture` | 6 |
  | Estate | 1 | 1 | 0 | 0 |
  | DNN | 7,842 | 7,805 | 37: 35 web links `- [url](url)`, 2 plain names `-
  CreateValidID` | 40 |

  The hypothesis fails on two counts:
  - 38 lines are other kinds of line the composer writes into the same section
  (its `href` and plain cases). No reference reading holds them.
  - 46 reference lines carry the author's words after ` — `. The (name, element,
  link) reading lacks them, and the stored `documentation` does not keep them
  either.

  No line in any corpus ran on over two lines, though the composer can write
  one.

  So both forms are kept, as you ruled for this case. Spec §4.3 now says:
  - why, with these counts;
  - that a viewer shows the lines and the graph reads only the references;
  - that a disagreement between them is the scanner's fault, which a reader
  cannot see.

  **What a check of their agreement would cost:** very little time (7,842 lines
  in DNN), but a second implementation of the scanner's documentation grammar in
  every reader. That is why it is not made.

  **Fork left to you:** the reading could grow to hold every kind of line the
  composer writes there (a reference with its words, a web link, a plain name).
  On these corpora those three kinds cover every line. The lines would then be
  held once, the way type references are, with the composer writing them on the
  engine's side. That changes the format to carry this section's whole shape,
  multi-line words included. I did not build it, since the blind readers go out
  next.

  ### 2. The blind cases, each seen failing
  - **First run:** taking each check out once left six checks whose cases still
  passed, because a second check caught the same file:
    - `wires-self` was still refused by the records-against-wires comparison;
    - `kind-7` and `strings-count-0` by the frames' order;
    - `paths-repeat` by the records-against-paths comparison;
    - the text cases `no-authored`, `entry-before-authored` and `two-cited-by`
  by the text being written again and compared;
    - the deflate end's writing-side check by the inflater's own reading side.

    As written, those cases proved only the pair of checks.
  - **Rewrite:** every case now asks for the words of the refusal its own check
  gives, and the two bundled tests are split into one per file. I added
  `paths-differ-from-records` for the one check no blind case reached. I also
  tied `symbol-before-entry` and `unknown-line` to "a file's lines begin with
  its file line".
  - **Second run:** with its own check taken out, each of the 26 binary and text
  cases fails. Taking out "an inflater's error is a refusal" on both sides
  fails `zlib-wrapped` and `after-deflate-end`.
  - **One check still has no case:** the writing-side check of the deflate
  stream's end. Node reports trailing bytes on the reading side too, so no file
  can show that check alone. I kept it for an inflater that reports on one side
  only.

  ### 3. The graph built only when asked
  `graphViewOf` gathers in one pass what each file needs of the others (its
  inbound and documented-by, both from targets alone), then builds a file's part
  only when it is asked for. `graphOf` is now that view asked for every file,
  so there is still one implementation.

  | DNN, interleaved, load 3.6 | First answer | Facts read and checked | Graph
  as asked (index plus the hub's Local Map) | Whole graph | Peak memory |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | Stored form | 76–83 ms | 553–595 ms | **620–645 ms** | 732–753 ms | 249–291
  MB |
  | `index.json.gz` | after reading it all | – | **600–664 ms** | – | 393–394 MB
  |

  So built as asked, the stored form reads DNN in JSON's time with every check
  made. It gives its first answer eight times sooner, in about 70 % of the
  memory. A consumer that asks for every file pays about 0.1 s more, building
  what JSON had already spelled out.

  On all three corpora, the graph still prints byte for byte as each
  `index.json`, and both round trips are exact, measured again after the change.

  ### 4. The browser tab, through the lane
  The page holds DNN's stored form (2.24 MB) as base64 in a block the browser
  does not run, with the module's built script inline and unchanged; the page is
  3.05 MB. It is opened from disk under a policy of one hashed script and no
  network. It took two holds, of 1.3 s and 4.8 s, and showed no errors.

  | DNN in a tab | First answer | Facts read and checked | Graph as asked |
  Whole graph | JS heap |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | Full speed | 55 ms | 394 ms | 434 ms | 559 ms | 60 MB |
  | Slowed 4× | 224 ms | 2.27 s | 2.53 s | 3.25 s | 58 MB |

  For comparison, the research's slowed tab took 3.2 s for JSON on DNN, but it
  also held the browser to two cores (`taskset`), which this run did not. I did
  not run a JSON page beside this one.

  ### Inventory: where the hypothesis holds and where it does not
  How it is built: the layout modules and their tests are untouched. One
  attribute, `data-reads="usesFirst"` on `#map-viewport`, makes the stylesheet
  reflect the content root and the wires' layer about the root's own edge. Each
  card and each directory's line is turned back to face the viewer with the

  and the looks.

  the root and the wires.

  the reflection.

  layout settings there are what a link carries. So it is sticky and no link
  carries it. The cost: Tuning's changed-count badge counts it, and Reset to
  defaults unmirrors.
  - **Cards face the viewer whole.** The header and controls keep their order
  and only the pins change sides. A closed directory keeps its folder tab on its
  own left.
  - **A directory's line keeps its reading order**, flush at the mirrored
  corner.
  - **What holds still on a press**: the card last acted on, else the subject,
  else the newest pin; with none, the middle of the view.

  ## Milestone 4: two commits; the read budget is deleted, and step 4's probe
  moves have not started

  Branch `worktree-agent-ac72d8a99b54c2e5d`. I fast-forwarded it to `f8ff639c4`,
  then added:
  - `a42c177ba`: resolve a package's `main` as Node does, held to Node's own
  resolver over every installed package.
  - `1250797e4`: delete the read budget, which no measure showed to buy
  anything; the copied head and the order of asks stay.

  ### 1. The read budget, measured in a page: deleted
  - **How it was measured:** `page-budget.mjs`, committed in my record, drives
  the page check's own scanning page through the lane under `taskset -c 0,1`,
  slowed 4× with `Emulation.setCPUThrottlingRate` as the scan-in-a-tab probe
  did. Memory is the page renderer's `VmHWM`. One lane hold per run.
  - **Folder:** DNN's Library, 1,413 files. The whole of DNN would not fit a
  five-minute hold at that pace. The Library's text is about 10 MB, so a 16 MiB
  budget would have kept everything; I ran 4 MiB instead.

  | Run (in order) | Load before → after | Time | Renderer peak | Reads |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | Keep everything | 4.9 → 4.3 | 57.1 s | 515 MB | 1,413 |
  | Keep nothing | 4.3 → 3.3 | 51.9 s | 514 MB | 4,233 |
  | Keep 4 MiB | 3.3 → 3.0 | 51.9 s | 509 MB | 3,239 |
  | Keep everything | 2.9 → 14.2 | 122.2 s | 512 MB | 1,413 |
  | Keep nothing | 12.4 → 6.7 | 68.5 s | 513 MB | 4,233 |

  - The last two ran beside my own lint and tests, hence their load.
  - **Result:** the peaks are within 6 MB of each other and the times follow the
  machine's load, not the budget. Keeping everything is no slower beyond that
  noise, and 515 MB is well within what a tab holds, so by your rule the budget
  goes.
  - **Deleted:** the budget, the generator's `readBudget` option, its default
  and its test cases.
  - **Kept:** the copy of each file's first bytes, and the order of asking for a
  file's reading before its first bytes.
  - **The overshoot (item 3)** went with the budget.
  - **One limit of this measure, written into the design:** the page check's
  page loads every file into memory before scanning, so reads there cost about
  as little as Node's warm-cache reads. A real page that reads each picked file
  when the scan asks would make every second read cost, which favours keeping
  everything even more.

  ### 2. The manifest reader against every installed package
  - **Oracle:** `tests/integration/live-docs/package-modules.oracle.test.ts`.
  For every package under `node_modules`, nested ones included (281 packages,
  Node 26.13.1), it asks Node's `require.resolve` and `import.meta.resolve` from
  the folder that holds the package, and compares with the reader's files. Both
  sides are counted as files on disk.
  - **Register:** `expected/package-modules.json` holds the count for each
  cause, with a reason for each. The test fails when a count moves.

  | Cause | Before the mend | After |
  |---|---|---|
  | Agree | 229 | 273 |
  | No `exports` and no `main`: Node takes `index.js` | 24 | 0 |
  | `main` without its extension, or naming a folder | 11 | 0 |
  | `main` names no file: Node falls back to `index.js` | 8 | 0 |
  | A nested copy: Node's ESM resolver answers with the top-level copy's file |
  6 | 6 |
  | The name of one of Node's own built-in modules (`punycode`,
  `string_decoder`) | 2 | 2 |

  - **The mend is Node's legacy resolution of `main`.** The reader tries, in
  order:
    1. the path `main` names
    2. that path with `.js`, `.json` or `.node`
    3. its `index` with each of those
    4. the package's own `index.*`

    The first that the workspace's listing holds is the entry. `packageModules`
  now returns Node's candidate list for each kind of consumer, one that imports
  and one that requires.
  - **Mixed keys in `exports`** (which Node refuses) occur in no installed
  package.
  - **Left open:** for the six nested copies, `import.meta.resolve` asked both
  from the holding folder and from inside the copy returns the top-level copy's
  file. For example, `eslint-visitor-keys` 5.0.1, nested under `eslint`,
  resolves to 3.4.3's `lib/index.js`. I could not establish why. The require
  side agrees for all six.
  - **One reader test touched:** in `json.test.ts`, the helper for "what a
  script runs" now filters to command entries only. With the mend, a manifest
  with no `main` and an `index.js` correctly gains that file as its module
  entry, and the old helper counted it as something the script runs.
  - **Docs:** no doc of this repository changed except the reader's and the
  oracle's.

› Message from general-purpose
  ## Fifth milestone: a directory is now a path's start and end at the command
  line, with one gap left so the page and the CLI share the rule

  I merged `8cfd27b85` first, plain and alone; it fast-forwarded. Then one
  commit:

  - `19b8994ad`: take a directory as a path's start and end at the command line,
  by one rule the page shares.

  **The gap:** the page's own copy of the directory rule still stands, in
  `views/localView/directory-state.ts` lines 28–30, a file you told me to stay
  out of. Until those three lines become an import, the rule exists twice. The
  exact edit is at the end.

  ### What the command line does now
  - **A directory as `--from` or `--to`** stands for every file under it, as the
  Explorer's path ends do. Trailing slash optional; matched in any case when
  not as spelled. Its label carries a slash, as `PaymentService/`.
    - The nearest file answers, told as asked. Estate: `Path from
  Portal/Pages/Default.aspx to PaymentService/App.config, 8 hop(s): each file
  uses the next.`
    - The other way round: `--from PaymentService --to Hub/App.config` gives
  `Path from PaymentService/App.config to Hub/App.config, 1 hop(s): each file is
  used by the next.`
  - **`--reachable` from a directory** lists what any of its files reaches,
  leaving out the directory's own files.
  - **A directory alone**, with neither `--to` nor `--reachable`, is refused in
  one line: `PaymentService is a directory: give --to for a path from any of its
  files, or --reachable for what they reach.`

  ### One rule for both surfaces
  Two pieces now live in `packages/engine/src/live-docs/pathfind.ts`:
  - `under` moved there from the Explorer.
  - `walkBetween` is new. It is the walk from one set of files to another, in
  which a file that is in both sets is not its own path. That exclusion was the
  page's private logic in `client/pathfind.ts`.

  The page and the command line now both call them:
  - The page's path search (`client/pathfind.ts`) calls `walkBetween`.
  - The history's path ends (`client/history/moment-paths.ts`) import `under`
  from the engine.
  - The command line's `searchGraph` takes a file or a set and walks through
  `walkBetween`.

  I touched no file under `views/localView/`.

  **To finish the move**,
  `packages/explorer/src/client/views/localView/directory-state.ts` lines 28–30
  should become `export { under } from
  "@live-documentation/engine/live-docs/pathfind";`. Its nine importers then

› Message from general-purpose
  ## Commit
  `418c0b821` on `worktree-agent-ad7e5ea604d827729`, from `8cfd27b85`. It adds:
  - `packages/stored-form/test/storedForm.review.test.ts`;
  - its Live Doc, with an authored Purpose and Notes;
  - `AI-Agent-Workspace/AgentSessionHistory/2026/11/2026-11-22/Probes/stored-for
  m-review/` (README plus probes and their JSON).

  Nothing in the module, the specification or the existing tests was touched.

  **Checks:**
  - Passed: `npm run lint`, `npm run build`, the three slopcop audits,
  `docs:links:enforce`, `live-docs:generate`.
  - The package's existing 48 cases (fuzzer included) pass at a load of 15. At a
  load of 51, "goes on from a stopped write" once timed out at 6.1 s against 5
  s. That is the case vitest.config already notes as load-sensitive.
  - **Red by the new file:** 15 failing, 1 passing (the base text the end-line
  cases are built from), 4 skipped with reasons.

  ## At the top: one guarantee broken five ways
  **"A reader's cost is bounded by the file it is handed"** (spec section 6:
  "Its time and memory are bounded by the file's size") breaks five ways, each
  with its own cause. By the rule, mending stops and the simplest design that
  doesn't need the guarantee gets measured.

  Every loop in this code is bounded by bytes already checked, as the module
  intends. What isn't bounded:
  - the product of two bounded loops (findings 1 and 3);
  - the constant (5);
  - work started before a check (4);
  - one platform call's limit (2).

  Two designs to measure (neither built):
  - Run any read, text export or graph of a stranger's file in a worker with a
  deadline and a memory ceiling, and treat running past either as a refusal.
  - Stream the text export and count bytes as they are written, instead of the
  names-first pre-walk. This is how both blind readers do it.

  ## Findings, ranked
  Each is a case in the review file (`npx vitest run
  packages/stored-form/test/storedForm.review.test.ts`). The probes beside the
  record measure them at larger sizes.

  **Breaks a guarantee**
  1. **The text writes a file's path on every line, but the bound counts the
  path once.**
     - File: path of 1,024 letters plus 20,000 empty symbols = 588 B.
     - `textOf` returns 22,402,209 bytes against a bound of 1,087,360, and does
  not refuse.
     - A 642-byte file (path of 32,768 letters, 18,000 symbols) makes `textOf`
  throw `RangeError: Invalid string length` at 725 MiB.
     - The text grows quadratically with what the file inflates to.
     - Breaks section 3.5 ("each string, once for every time a record names it")
  and section 6. The text is at fault too: its count doesn't count what it
  writes.
  2. **The text of one file of 149,000 lines throws.** File: 1,886 B. `textOf`
  rejects with `RangeError: Maximum call stack size exceeded`, from
  `lines.push(...fileLines(f))` in `textOfScan`. Breaks section 6: "never fails
  in any other way".
  3. **The graph sorts paths with `comparePaths` from the first letter.** Setup:
  n files whose paths share L letters, each depending on all n. Cost is about
  n² log n × L.

     | n | L | File | `readScan` | `graphViewOf`, one file asked | `graphOf` |
     | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
     | 1,024 | 8,192 | 0.24 MiB | 4.4 s | 157 s | 212 s |
     | 200 (in the test) | 4,096 | 5,212 B | 236 ms | | 4,490 ms |

     - Asking for one file costs the whole pre-pass.
     - Breaks section 6, and section 5's "one pass over the targets".
  4. **The whole read starts inflating every frame before it checks anything.**
  Setup: k records frames of 1 byte each, with a lying paths count. Both readers
  refuse it.

     | k | File | `openScan` refuses in | `readScan` refuses in | `readScan`
  peak (RSS) |
     | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
     | 10,000 | 0.5 MiB | 22 ms | 2.5 s | |
     | 100,000 | 4.6 MiB | 266 ms | 97 s | 2,032 MiB |

     - Time grows faster than the file.
     - The fuzzer's memory measure saw 1,049 MiB against its own bound of 341
  MiB. It doesn't count the inflaters' native memory.
  5. **Memory per byte of file.** Setup: one record of empty symbols, padded
  with empty stored blocks to sit within the inflation bound.
     - 428 KB file: 1,055 MiB peak, 764 MiB held.
     - 94 KB file (in the test): 181 MB held, against the fuzzer's 54 MB bound.
     - Section 3.5's "every fact … one uncompressed byte, so memory is bounded
  by the file's size" holds in order but not in size. It is about 25 bytes of
  JavaScript per raw byte, times 64.
  6. **The text's end line is accepted written six other ways:** `[null, "end",
  2]`, `2.0`, `2e0`, `end`, a trailing CR, leading/trailing whitespace.
  `parseText` only parses the end line and never rewrites and compares it.
  Breaks section 8's "written in exactly one way", "no line holds a CR" and its
  refused list.
  7. **A file named `__proto__`.** `graphOf` keys files in a plain object.
     - `Object.keys(graph.files)` is `["a"]`, though the view has `__proto__`.
     - `a.inbound` names a file the graph doesn't hold.
     - `graph.files["edges"]` returns `__proto__`'s edges.

     The engine's `deriveLiveDocGraph` has the same keying. From reading only
  (outside scope, not run): it would throw for a file named `constructor` or
  `toString`.

  **Gaps between the text and the module**

  8. **A text no valid file holds is read.** One authored string of 64 MiB:
  `scanOfText` accepts it, `bytesOfText` refuses it. Section 8's list of checks
  omits `MAX_SECTION`, while its "Equivalently, … the text a reader writes for
  some valid file" excludes this text. (Test.)
  9. **Section 5 says "each dependency with a link" makes an edge.** The module
  and the engine make one for every dependency. No blind reader ever compared
  graphs. (Skipped: the text is at fault.)
  10. **Section 2.4 says a link "may not name an absolute place".** Its rule
  stores `https://`, `javascript:`, `data:`, `""` and a link holding NUL.
  (Skipped: text.)
  11. **The spec's claim to have been read blind is stale.**
      - The blind readers were committed at 02:56 (`d7b7ec38f`).
      - `docReference`, the `documentation` member and the documentation
  relation came at 03:38 (`7ba7acb27`), along with the extension's refusal of
  `\` and NUL.
      - Neither reader contains "documentation".

  **Weaknesses of the tests**

  12. **The fuzzer almost never reaches a section's grammar.**
      - Of its 800 mutants, all but 7 are refused at the frame layer: kind, cut,
  limits, order, inflate or hash. The 7 are reached through a lying header
  field.
      - None reaches marks, string ids, enumerations, varint form, path or link
  rules, record order, or paths/wires against records.
      - Its worker never calls `openScan`, `file(i)`, `graphOf`, `graphViewOf`,
  `scanOfText` or `bytesOfText`.
      - It has two bases, and never a valid but costly file.
      - (Test: 2 of 350 mutants reach the grammar, where a tenth is asked.)
  13. **Checks with no case.** Taking each of the 91 refusals out once, in a
  copy, fails no unit or blind case for 56 of them. Fourteen of these were rerun
  with the fuzzer too, and it caught none:
      - first-met string order;
      - the end's name check;
      - the reason and role ranges;
      - target-without-link, anchor-without-target, entry symbol link without
  its symbol;
      - NUL in a path;
      - numbers past 2^32 − 1 or of six bytes;
      - the file inflation bound;
      - the text bound;
      - the string-twice check and the wires-vs-links check. Their existing
  cases accept either of two regexes, so they pass with their own check removed.

      `raw-fuzz.mjs` is the generator for most of these. I did not add cases,
  since a check that refuses today would pass.

  **Remarks**

  14. **What a hostile file can make a person see.** Example: dependency `./b`,
  linking to b's doc, with target `src/c.ts`.
      - The pane shows a use of b. The graph wires a→c, and b has no inbound, so
  b looks like dead code.
      - A documentation line naming Widget on b, whose reading targets c, makes
  c "documented by" a.
      - Links can climb anywhere (`../../../../etc/passwd`) and can be
  `javascript:`.
      - Names can hold bidirectional controls: `gnp.exe` shows as `exe.png`.
      - The engine's `linkTarget` is about 15 lines; whether the format checks
  links is section 12's open question.
  15. **Windows.** Stored as allowed: `a\..\..\x`, `CON`, `aux.txt`,
  `src/a:b.txt`, `dir./x`, `x.`, `...`, `.. /x`. Section 9 doesn't name three
  hazards:
      - an empty location (or extension `.ts` with files `a` and `a.ts`) puts a
  document on a source file;
      - extension `:x` makes `src/a.ts:x`, an alternate data stream on NTFS;
      - names that are equal after normalization (NFC vs NFD) collide on macOS.

      The extension refuses `\` while paths may hold it. Outside scope: the
  engine's `linkTarget` with an empty extension resolves every link to `""`.
  16. **An empty link.** The module's edge carries `link: ""`; the engine drops
  it.

  ## Attacked and not broken
  - **One file, one map; one name (binary).**
    - Read every check against the spec.
    - `raw-fuzz.mjs`: 5,000 mutants made below the hashes across 5 bases, in 9
  ways, re-framed with correct hashes. 758 accepted, 4,242 refused by 65
  distinct checks.
    - Every accepted file, rewritten from its facts, gave the same content name
  and the same text: no second name.
    - The blind Python reader read the 2,000 symbol-free mutants. It disagreed
  on 2, both the extension rule it predates.
  - **Path order:** 117,649 pairs drawn from boundary code points, all agreeing
  with UTF-8 byte order.
  - **Documentation relation:** read against section 5 (self-references,
  unscanned targets, link/target/anchor presence). No difference found, and
  nothing reaches edges or wires.

  ## Not reached
  - A browser tab: Chromium's `DecompressionStream` backpressure, and the tab's
  heap on findings 4 and 5.
  - The Go reader as an oracle (as stale as the Python one).
  - RFC 1951 corner-case splits between inflaters.
  - Hostile `resumeFrom` inputs.

  ## Commands the harness refused
  One: `grep … ; git -C . log …` (chained, naming git with a runtime-computed
  directory). Redone as plain single commands.

  No subagent used. Scratch folder and `node_modules` link removed.

  that way;

  ### Results so far: Python reader, snapshot of its `reader.py` taken at 05:01
  UTC
  The comparison keeps the first reading's limits and adds `--graph`. In the
  table, "agree" means both readers gave the same text byte for byte, the same
  content name, or both refused.

  | Corpus | Read | Name | Graph |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | Corpora (this repository with 1,334 files, the estate, DNN with 10,777) |
  3/3 | 3/3 | 0/3 |
  | Made scans not shown (11, including `made-doc-references` and
  `made-no-wires`) | 11/11 | 11/11 | 4/11 |
  | Mutants, unit cases' non-canonical files, first record's own, its texts (446
  files) | 446/446 (438 both refuse, 7 same, plus the 65.7 MB past-the-bound
  text written byte for byte as `textOfScan`) | | 2/8 |
  | Files aimed at the amended sections (20) | 20/20 (17 both refuse, 3 same) |
  | 0/1 |

  The 20 aimed files cover:
  - the extension holding `\`, `.md\x`, U+0000 or `/` (binary) and `\` (text),
  and the empty extension;
  - lines misordered: authored before file, dependency before symbol, entry
  after symbol, scanner before location;
  - lines doubled: file, authored, cited-by and location are refused, and a
  doubled symbol is valid because it is two symbols;
  - a symbol with no `documentation`, a reference with a target but no link, its
  members reordered, `"element":null`;
  - a valid text past the export's bound.

  All 17 graph disagreements have one cause. `gdiff.mts` checks each one: with
  the module's edges for dependencies that have no link removed, every graph
  agrees byte for byte in sorted form, `documentation` and `documentedBy`
  included. That covers DNN, the estate, and every documentation-reference shape
  in `made-doc-references`: with and without element, link, target and anchor,
  to its own file, and to a file outside the scan. The module makes 6,576 such
  edges for DNN, 1,405 for this repository and 15 for the estate.

  ### Disagreements and their class
  1. **The text is at fault: section 5 says two different things about a
  dependency without a link.**
     - "**The links that make edges**, in order: each dependency with a link,
  then, for each symbol in order, each reference in order."
     - Against that: "`link`, when there is one." A type's edge always has a
  link, so this sentence can only be about a dependency's edge.
     - The module makes an edge for every dependency, linked or not
  (`{"kind":"import","label":"node:fs"}`), as this repository's `index.json`
  does.
     - The Python reader followed the first sentence. Read plainly, the text
  supports the reader.
     - Suggested change to the text: "each dependency, with a link or not", if
  the engine's edges are meant.
  2. **The module is at fault, in a small way: it names the wrong line in some
  text refusals.** The verdicts are right. For `text-4.0-b` the faulty line is
  4, but the module says "line 6" and quotes from line 4 on. For `text-4.0` the
  faulty line is 5, and the module says 16.

  ## Engine review of day/2026-11-22: first milestone

  The review is committed as **`0d780aacf`** on branch
  `worktree-agent-a33555ec369858a9e`, from `46e13bf60`. It adds four test files,
  red today, each with a Live Doc and an authored Purpose and Notes. The record
  is `AI-Agent-Workspace/AgentSessionHistory/2026/11/2026-11-22/Probes/engine-r
  eview/README.md`, with the counting script `count-host.mts` beside it. No
  product code and no existing test was touched.

  No guarantee broke twice by different means.

  ### Findings, in rank order

  **1. A wrong answer: a path between two ends that share files is lost.**
  - **Input:** `src/a.ts` uses `src/b.ts`, and `src/Pay/p.ts` uses `src/a.ts`.
  - **Command:** `inspect --from src/ --to src/b.ts`
  - **Answer:** "No path runs between src/ and src/b.ts, either way.", exit 1.
  `--from src --to src/Pay` gives the same.
  - In `--json`, the target is listed among `visited`, and `src/a.ts` is called
  "terminal".
  - **Why it is wrong:** `walkBetween`'s rule is that "a file in both sets is
  not its own path". The code does more: it drops every start from the targets.
  So a path between two different files is lost whenever its target is also a
  start.
  - The Explorer walks through the same function, so the page gives the same
  wrong answer.
  - **Test:** `packages/engine/src/live-docs/pathfind.overlapping-ends.test.ts`.

  **2. A wrong answer: the reader of `package.json` disagrees with Node on
  shapes no installed package holds.** Each was built as a package and asked of
  Node both ways. Test:
  `packages/engine/src/live-docs/adapters/json.exports-held-by-node.test.ts`,
  all 7 red.

  | Manifest | Node | Reader |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | `exports: null` beside `main: "./lib/main.js"` | `lib/main.js` (Node reads
  `exports` only when "not null or undefined"; the code tests `=== undefined`) |
  nothing |
  | `.`: `{node:{import:null, require:"./n.cjs"}, default:"./d.js"}` | `n.cjs`;
  import is not exported | also `d.js` |
  | `.`: `{import:[], require, default}` | `r.cjs`; import is not exported |
  also `d.js` |
  | `.`: `{import:[null], require, default}` | `r.cjs`; import is not exported |
  also `d.js` |
  | `exports: "./../outside.js"` | `ERR_INVALID_PACKAGE_TARGET` | an entry
  outside the package |
  | `exports: "./lib/../x.js"` | `ERR_INVALID_PACKAGE_TARGET` | `x.js` |
  | `{".": "./a.js", "import": "./b.js"}` | `ERR_INVALID_PACKAGE_CONFIG` |
  `b.js` |

  In the three nested-`null` cases, `exportTarget` turns the inner `null` into
  `undefined` and goes on to the next condition, where Node stops.

  **3. A wrong answer: a directory typed in the wrong case stands for two
  directories.**
  - **Input:** a workspace with `src/Pay/`, `src/pay/` and
  `src/PaymentService/`.
  - **Command:** `inspect --from src/PAY --to src/PaymentService --json`
  - **Answer:** exit 0, `kind: "path"` from `src/pay/q.ts`, labelled `src/PAY/`.
  - **Why:** `resolveEnd` folds the case of every path, so the end becomes the
  union of both directories.
  - **Test:** `scripts/live-docs/inspect.case-folded-directory.test.ts`, 1 red
  and 1 green. It proposes that the command fail and name both spellings; the
  builder may choose another honest form.

  **4. A gap between rule and code: a symbol's path to itself is a path.**
  - **Command:** `inspect --from 'src/a.ts#a' --to 'src/a.ts#a'`
  - **Answer:** "Symbol path ... (0 hop(s), outbound)", exit 0.
  - **Why:** the file search obeys the day's rule that a file is no path to
  itself; `searchSymbolPath` does not.
  - **Test:** a case in the path-finder test above. It is yours to rule whether
  a symbol's path to itself is a different question.

  **5. A gap between rule and code: the host is asked again and again for absent
  `go.mod` files.**
  - **Command:** `count-host.mts` on `go/depot`.
  - **Answer:** 32 failed host reads of eight `go.mod` files that do not exist;
  `report/go.mod` and `stock/go.mod` are asked 8 times each.
  - **Why:** `moduleOf` walks up by reading, and a failed read is kept by no
  one, so every Go file asks again.
  - Step 4 of the design is meant to move these probes; nothing measures them
  meanwhile.
  - **Test:** `packages/engine/src/live-docs/adapters/go.module-probe.test.ts`,
  red.

  **6. A weakness of the tests: the page check counts no failed read.**
  `bytesOf` in `tests/page-scan/stand-ins/fs.mjs` counts a read only once it
  finds the file, so "read once" cannot see finding 5. No test file holds this:
  the mend belongs in the check itself.

  **7. A weakness of the tests: the package oracle's import side never asks from
  a nested copy.**
  - Node reads `import.meta.resolve(name, parent)`'s second argument only under
  `--experimental-import-meta-resolve`.
  - So `importedByNode` resolves every package from the test's own folder.
  - **Shown by:** `node --input-type=module -e 'console.log(import.meta.resolve(
  "eslint-visitor-keys","file:///nonexistent/x.mjs"))'` gives the top-level
  copy. With the flag, the nested copy is found.
  - So the register's cause "a nested copy ... Not established why" is the
  oracle's own mistake. The six nested packages' import side has never been
  compared.
  - Also, `causeOf` files any disagreement in a nested package under that one
  cause.
  - **Mend:** run with the owner's folder as the working folder, or pass the
  flag.

  ### Red by my files
  Thirteen cases are red: 4 in the path-finder test, 7 in the manifest test, 1
  in the case-folding test and 1 in the Go test. Two are green.

  `npm run lint`, `npm run build`, `slopcop:markdown`, `slopcop:assets`,
  `slopcop:symbols` and `docs:links:enforce` all exit 0.

    - A file shorter than the kept head.

    - Seven more samples (`tidepool`, `python/ledger`, `rust/analytics`,

  - **Manifests:** these agree with Node.
    - A list holding a condition object.
    - A `null` in a fall-back list.

    - A `main` naming a folder that has its own manifest.
    - Three deep ending in a `null`: the import side is wrong, but the two sides
  together equal Node's answer, which is what the product shows.
  - **crefs**, run through the reader only, without the compiler:
    - Correct links for overloads by `int`, `Int32` and `System.Int32`, a nested
  type, generics of one and two parameters, and both alias forms.

  ## Fifth milestone, first leg: B, E and H done and C measured; D's rule needs
  your ruling; the A designs, F and G are not started

  **Commit:** `5cec6d384` "Mend the stored form's faults an independent review
  found, and state that every dependency makes an edge". It is on
  `worktree-agent-a5522d16dedb937a3`. Before it I merged `46e13bf60` and then
  the reviewer's branch (`418c0b821`), both plain and with no conflicts. I read
  the review's record and its test file whole first. The record is appended
  under "Fifth milestone, first leg" in `Probes/stored-form-module/README.md`.

  ### B, the plain faults, mended (no review case touched)
  - **1 and 2, the text:**
    - It is written line by line and counted as written, in UTF-8 bytes with
  line breaks included.
    - Before a line is built, the bytes its path and names will take are
  counted, so no line is built past the bound.
    - There is no spread call any more.
    - The bound is now on the text itself (§3.5).
  - **3, the graph's sorts:** wires, inbound and documented-by are file numbers
  sorted as numbers. Each target is looked up once by its path.
  - **4, the whole read:**
    - Each section is inflated only when it is read, after the checks on every
  section before it.
    - Each frame's count is checked against its raw length from the header
  alone, before anything is inflated: string 1 byte, record 7, path 2, wires 1.
  §2.1 lists these.
  - **6, the end line:** written again and compared, like every other line.
  - **7, prototype names:** the graph's files are keyed in an object with no
  prototype. A new case holds that `__proto__`, `constructor`, `toString` and
  `hasOwnProperty` are each in the graph and wired, and that a path no file has
  answers nothing. It failed against the module before this mend.
  - **8, the section limit:** a text's facts are sized into the sections the
  layout would hold before the text counts as valid. §8's list says so.

  **Result:** 13 of the review's 15 failing cases now pass. The two still
  failing are finding 5 (memory a byte costs once read, which waits on A) and
  finding 12 (fuzzer reach, which is F).

  **Corpora, checked again after B:** on this repository (1,336 files), the
  estate (41) and DNN (10,777), the graph read back still prints byte for byte
  as each `index.json`, and both round trips are exact.

  **Fork from B4:** strict in-order inflation gives back the last milestone's
  lead.
  - At a load of 20, interleaved, DNN's facts took 3.2–3.3 s against JSON's
  2.2–2.7 s. On a quiet machine last milestone it was 553–595 ms against 600–664
  ms.
  - Not yet measured quiet.
  - A window of a few sections inflated ahead would bound the wasted work by a
  constant and keep most of the concurrency. Your call: that window, or strictly
  nothing inflated ahead of its check.

  ### E and H
  - **E:** §5 now says every dependency makes an edge, with a link or not; one
  without a link has no `link` and no `to`. It keeps a note of the old sentence
