  ### A
  §6's sentence on cost is marked in the text as not holding and under redesign.
  The marking names the five breaks, the four now mended, and the two designs
  to be measured. **Neither design is measured yet.**

  ### C, measured (`link-facts.ts`)

  | Corpus | Links carrying a target | Stored equals the engine's rule | Given
  no target | Deriving every target | File without targets |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | This repository | 9,153 | 9,153 | 0 | 43 ms | −1.7 % |
  | Estate | 177 | 177 | 0 | 1 ms | −4.0 % |
  | DNN | 48,689 | 48,689 | 0 | 269 ms | −5.1 % |

  - The probe's sink makes targets with `linkTarget`, so the agreement holds by
  construction. What the numbers show is that every link derives. A real sink's
  agreement can only be measured once the two are wired.
  - Both choices (derive, or store both and refuse where they differ) need the
  rule in the reader, so the numbers favour deriving, as you lean. It is **not

  - The 269 ms is the engine's functions as written, which build a document path
  per link. Caching by folder and link should cut it; not measured.

  ### D, measured

  45,122) and fragments (1,011 / 0 / 3,567). No link field holds a scheme
  anywhere, which confirms that every web link is in documentation lines.
  - **Your rule as written cannot be taken.** "Nothing climbing above the docs'
  root" would refuse every source link (39,086 in DNN) and every cited-by link,
  because those climb out of the documents to the scanned folder's own files.

  no scheme, no NUL. I have not measured it or refused anything yet.

  ## Fifth milestone, second leg: one red case remains, so the branch is not
  ready to merge

  The review's twelfth case is now green. Its fifth is still red, and waits on
  design A. So 14 of the review's 15 cases pass.

  **Commit:** `a272c52e4` "Give the stored form's fuzzer a stage below the
  hashes, and measure inflating sections ahead", on
  `worktree-agent-a5522d16dedb937a3`, on top of `5cec6d384`. The record is under
  "Fifth milestone, second leg" in `Probes/stored-form-module/README.md`.

  **What held:**
  - `npm run lint`, the three audits, `docs:links:enforce`, and
  `live-docs:generate` with its `--check` all pass.
  - The whole unit project, run once: 3,738 passed, 4 skipped. The one failure
  is the review's fifth case.
  - No command was refused. The scratch folder is removed.

  ### F: the review's twelfth case is green
  - **The new stage:** `test/fuzz/mutants.mjs` now has a second stage, which is
  the review's raw-section fuzzer taken in. On every odd seed except a bomb's,
  one section's uncompressed bytes are changed in one of nine ways. The file is
  then set down again with every hash and the end right, so the mutant reaches
  that section's grammar.
  - **The worker:** it gains the review's oracle. When a reader accepts a file
  and can write it, the facts are written again and read back; a different
  content name is a fault ("a second name").
  - **The harness:** it now goes on after a hang, from the next seed in a fresh
  worker. It used to stop at the first hang, and with the new stage the careless
  reader hung at seed 1 and hid every fault after it.
  - **Results:**
    - The review's "reaches the grammar of a section" case passes; it failed
  before.
    - The real reader returns facts or a refusal for all 800 mutants, within its
  bounds, with no second name.
    - The careless reader is still caught in at least two ways.

  ### B4: inflating ahead, measured
  `window.mjs` times the whole read strictly in order against a window. The
  window inflates ahead only sections whose headers have passed every
  header-only check, with the raw bytes in flight held under a constant. I ran 7
  runs each, interleaved. The machine was never quiet: other agents held the
  load at 11–17.

  | Median | Strict | 8 MiB window | Strict | 32 MiB window |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | DNN | 1,345 ms | 927 ms | 816 ms | 655 ms |
  | Five times DNN | 2,395 ms | 2,241 ms | 2,847 ms | 2,336 ms |

  - **Ruling outcome:** strict is not within the noise. It is 20–30 % slower on
  DNN in both pairs and 6–18 % slower at five times. So by your ruling the
  window is to be built, with its waste stated as its constant: at most the
  window's raw bytes inflated ahead of the section being checked.
  - **Not built yet, nor its case.** A case that holds the bound needs a hostile
  file whose refusal comes only in its records, after valid paths and wires for
  every file the records declare. With compressible sections, the inflation
  bound refuses such a file before it reaches the records.
  - **The case I would build:** a few incompressible 1 MiB records sections with
  the first one refused, holding that what is inflated ahead stays under the
  constant, measured as memory.
  - **Fork for you:** I would set the window at 8 MiB unless you prefer 32 MiB
  (more speed for more memory).

  **Writes from disk for these measures:** DNN 2.4 s at a 206 MB peak (2,238,498
  bytes); five times DNN 8.3 s at 251 MB (6,427,108 bytes).

  ### Not reached in this leg

  3. D built: the scanned-folder rule measured, stated and refused.

  ## Engine review, second pass: two new wrong answers and one more blind spot
  in the tests

  The second pass is committed as **`77f47429c`** on
  `worktree-agent-a33555ec369858a9e`. Before it I merged `bcd44843b` with a
  plain merge; it did not conflict (`20f81043c`). The record continues in
  `AI-Agent-Workspace/AgentSessionHistory/2026/11/2026-11-22/Probes/engine-revie
  w/README.md`, under the heading "2026-11-22, later: the second pass". No
  product code or existing test was changed.

  No guarantee broke twice by different means.

  ### Findings, in rank order

  **1. A wrong answer: the reverse search's "no path" claims something it never
  tested.**
  - **Input:** a copy of the estate sample.
  - **Command:** `inspect --from PaymentService --to Contracts --direction both`
  - **Answer:** `REVERSE PATH (inbound): No path found.` then `Nothing that
  depends on "PaymentService" also depends on "Contracts".`
  - **Why it is wrong:** `Hub/App.config` uses `PaymentService/App.config` and
  `Contracts/IPaymentService.cs`; `inspect --from Hub/App.config --direction
  outbound` lists both. The inbound search only shows that `Contracts` does not
  depend on `PaymentService`.
  - The sentence (`emitDualDirectionResult`, `inspect/emit.ts:540`) is older
  than the day. Since the day's directory ends, it names a directory by its last
  segment.
  - **Test:** `scripts/live-docs/inspect.reverse-path-wording.test.ts`, red. It
  first checks the fact it relies on through the command line's own neighbours.

  **2. A wrong answer: a `cref` to one overload links two overloads, or one it
  does not name.** I built two projects and ran `csharp-compiler.cs` (Roslyn
  4.12) on exactly the test's files.

  | cref | Compiler | Reader |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | `Lib.Calc.Mix(Lib.Calc[])` | `Mix(Calc[])` | `Mix(Calc[])` and `Mix(ref
  Calc)` |
  | `Lib.Calc.Mix(ref Lib.Calc)` | `Mix(ref Calc)` | both |
  | `Lib.Calc.Mix(Lib.Pair{Lib.Calc})` | nothing | both one-parameter overloads
  |
  | `Lib.Calc.Mix(Lib.Calc,)` | nothing | `Mix(Calc, Calc)` |

  - **First cause:** `parameterShape` drops array marks and modifiers, so
  `Calc[]` and `ref Calc` look alike.
  - **Second cause:** when no overload matches, `namedMembers` links every
  overload with the same number of parameters (`matching.length > 0 ? matching :
  counted`), so a guess is shown as a link.
  - **Test:**
  `packages/engine/src/live-docs/adapters/csharp.cref-overloads.test.ts`, 4 red
  and a control (`Mix(Lib.Calc, Lib.Calc)`) green.

  **3. A weakness of the tests: the C# oracle's documentation comparison cannot
  see a wrong declaration.**
  - **Command:** `npm run oracle:csharp -- <those two projects> --detail`
  - **Answer:** "2 pairs, 2 of them the compiler's", with nothing only the
  compiler or only the reader makes, while the four references above link wrong
  declarations.
  - **Why:** `compareDocumentation` compares file pairs and drops same-file
  references. A wrong overload, a wrong member in the right file, and every
  same-file reference are invisible to it, on DNN and mojoPortal too.
  - **Also:** a `cref` Roslyn cannot parse (`L::Calc`) is missing from the
  compiler's record. If the reader linked one, the oracle would file it under
  "unwritten", the cause meant for inactive preprocessor sections.
  - No test file: the mend is to compare declarations; finding 2 is the
  product's test.

  **4. A weakness of the tests: the parity ledger reads neither the exit status
  nor which stream.** `run` in `parity.test.ts` joins standard output and error
  and resolves on close whatever the status. So a "yes" row passes if its
  fragment appears anywhere, an error message included, even when the command
  fails. I found this by reading only: every "yes" row exits 0 today, so I had
  no input to show it.

  ### Red by my files
  On the merged code, 18 cases are red across 6 files: the 13 from the first
  pass plus 4 and 1 from this one. Three are green. `npm run lint`, `npm run
  build`, the three slopcop audits and `docs:links:enforce` all exit 0.

  ### Attacked and held
  - **Readers' probes on the listing.** I asked the HTML, CSS, Python and Ruby
  readers twice, once with the run's listing and once with the disk. They gave
  the same answers for listed files and folders, Python package and namespace
  imports, and Ruby `require_relative`. I compared full scans at the base and
  the tip on made workspaces. Two answers differ, and in both the base was
  already worse, so neither is the listing's fault:
    - **Links to documents:** HTML `href`s to `README.md`, `guide.md` and
  `LICENSE`, and a CSS `url()` to an `.md` file. The base drew broken links to
  `README.md.md`, a doc that does not exist; the tip shows unlinked names,
  because documents are kept out of the listing.
    - **Includes through symbolic links:** a link to a folder inside the
  workspace and one outside. The base linked to docs that do not exist; the tip
  shows them unlinked, though the file is in the listing as `third_party/x.h`.
    - The honest link is in neither version. These are remarks, with no tests.
  - **The author's reference against the compiler.** These agree, declaration by
  declaration:
    - `int`, `Int32` and `System.Int32`;
    - `Echo{T}(T)` beside `Echo(int)`;
    - nested-type members from outside and from within, including the outer
  `Calc.Run` named from inside the nested type;
    - generics of one and two parameters, and `Pair{,}`;
    - `global::`, the alias `L.Pair{X}`, and `Run ()` with a space.
    - **`L::Calc`:** Roslyn does not parse it and the reader links nothing, so
  they agree.
    - **Known classes:** the operator (the oracle's `notRead` class) and
  parameters that need the framework.
  - **The command line, the same input twice.** I ran 25 invocations twice each
  over the estate copy: `inspect` in every mode with and without `--json`, five
  malformed calls, `board`, `lint` and `find-orphans`. All were identical to the
  byte in output, errors and exit status. Every malformed call exits 1 with a
  message, and none waits for input.
  - **The JSON against the text.** Three answers agree apart from finding 1. One
  remark: for a route between directories, `kind: "path"` reports the end files
  as `from`/`to` while `kind: "dual-direction"` reports the directories asked,
  and the first one's text never mentions the directories.

  ### Not reached

  - These registers: the PowerShell and SQL ledgers, and the page check's

  - Each answer against its stated bound.

  ## Second blind reading of the stored form: finished

  Both blind readers, Python and Go, give the module's answer on every file for

  `documentedBy` included, differ from the module's for one reason only: the
  text's fault in section 5. Running the module over the readers' own test files
  turned up one new fault in the module.

  **Commits on my branch:**
  - `d2df64c46` was the interim state.
  - `4fb88be5d` finishes the reading.

  Everything is in `AI-Agent-Workspace/AgentSessionHistory/2026/11/2026-11-22/Pr
  obes/stored-form-second-reader/`. The new section is "Second reading, of the
  amended specification" in its README. I also changed the harness
  (`blind-implementer.mjs`, `samples.ts`, `compare.mjs`) and added `gdiff.mts`,
  `edges.mts`, `module-answers-2.json`, `shown-2/`, `runs-2/`,
  `python-reader-2/` and `go-reader-2/`. `docs:links:enforce` and the three
  slopcop audits are clean.

  ### The runs
  - **Python:** 74.0 minutes, 67 turns, $9.47. `reader.py` is 2,118 lines, with
  15 questions and 129 lines of refusals. Its final `reader.py` is byte for byte
  the 05:53 copy I compared.
  - **Go:** 34.6 minutes, 49 turns, $6.30. Four Go files, 2,683 lines, with 20
  questions and 138 lines of refusals.
  - **The stopped JavaScript run:** no cost reported. It was not compared,
  because Node does not start under the comparison's address-space limit.
  - **Blindness checks:** $0.74 for the three.
  - **Total reported: $16.51.**

  Both programs are committed unchanged. I left out the files their tests build
  (each reader's `make_cases.py` rebuilds them), Go's binary and 45 MB build
  cache, and Python's `__pycache__`.

  ### Agreement by corpus
  The same counts hold for both readers.

  | Corpus | `read` and `name` | `graph` |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | The three corpora (this repository, the estate, DNN) | 3 and 3 | 0 of 3 |
  | Made scans not shown | 11 and 11 | 4 of 11 |
  | The 446 mutants, unit-case files, first reading's own files and texts | 7
  the same, 438 refused by both, 0 disagreements | 2 of 8 |
  | My 20 files aimed at the amended sections | 3 the same, 17 refused by both |
  0 of 1 |
  | The six edge files I made after the Go reader's questions | 5 the same, 1
  refused by both | 5 of 5 |

  On the file whose text is past the export bound, Python writes the text byte
  for byte as `textOfScan`. Go refuses the text and says it is refusing the
  text, not the file. Section 3.5 allows both.

  **Each reader's own test files.** All three programs refuse every case the
  other reader made to be refused. The module refuses all of Go's 159 cases and
  186 of Python's 188; the two it accepts are finding 2.

  **One run past a limit, not the reader's fault.** Go's `name` of
  `made-seeded-11-773.txt` died while the Go runtime was starting, under a 1,185
  MiB address-space limit. Run again under 1,024, 1,300 and 2,048 MiB, it gives
  the module's name. The first reading recorded the same behaviour.

  ### Disagreements, each classed
  1. **Against the text: section 5 says two things about a dependency without a
  link.**
     - "**The links that make edges**, in order: each dependency with a link, …"
     - Against that: "`link`, when there is one." A type's edge always has a
  link, so this sentence can only describe a dependency's edge.
     - The module makes an edge for every dependency, as `index.json` does.
     - Both readers followed the first sentence, each on its own, and neither
  asked about it.
     - `gdiff.mts` checks every graph that differs (18 for Python, 18 for Go).
  Each one matches once the module's edges for unlinked dependencies are taken
  out: 6,576 of them for DNN, 1,405 for this repository.
  2. **Against the module: it reads a text's end line by its value, not its
  written form.** Section 8.1 says: "There is no whitespace between any two
  tokens", and "Only these forms are written, and no other is accepted." The
  module accepts Python's `text-space.txt` (`[null, "end",0]`) and
  `text-trailing-space.txt`. On `everyFact`'s text it also accepts `[null,"end",
  3]`, `[ null,"end",3]` and `[null,"end",3.0]`. It refuses the same kind of
  change in the format, location, scanner and left-out lines.
  3. **Against the module, small: some text refusals name the wrong line.** For
  `text-4.0-b` it says line 6 when the fault is on line 4. For `text-4.0` it
  says 16 for line 5, and for head lines it says line 5.
  4. **Against the text: the text bound counts names two ways (section 3.5).**
  "the names it would write" conflicts with "each string, once for every time a
  record names it". The text writes a file's path on every line, but a record
  names it once. The module counts per record (65,544,891 for
  `probe-text-past-the-file-bound`), Go counts per line (65,546,892). No sample
  falls between the two counts.
  5. **Against the text, by silence: nothing bounds the graph.** Section 6
  bounds a reader's time by the file's size, and section 3.5 says a file past
  the text bound still gives its graph. Go's 442-byte
  `valid-past-text-bound.fpk` gives a graph holding each of its long labels as
  an edge.

  The readers' other choices where the text is silent are listed in the README
  as finding 6. They are the same in both readers, and the module agrees
  wherever a file reaches them: `MAX_SECTION` applied to a text, the first path
  sharing nothing, the format line checked, what a path or link may hold
  (`a\b.ts`, an empty link, U+0000). Two of them no file here reaches.

  ### Their questions, grouped
  The README has them in full. The shared themes:
  - **The bounds:** how the text bound counts, whether a text past it is refused
  or written, the graph having no bound, `MAX_SECTION` for a text, what
  "bounded by the file's size" means in practice.
  - **The graph:** `edges`, `outbound` and `inbound` always present, `from` and
  `parameter` both on a parameter's edge, "hold `re-export`" read as exactly,
  `toSymbol` only with `to`, an empty anchor giving `"toSymbol":""`.
  - **Paths and links:** what a link may hold, a target checked as a path, the
  first path's `shared`, head strings kept out of the string table.
  - **Reading:** which inflater decides deflate's corner cases (zlib and
  `compress/flate`, unmeasured against each other), which reason wins, optional
  early refusals, the format line, a records frame without a strings frame,
  Provenance sentences read as history.
  - **The brief's own gap:** the exit status.

  ### Blindness, and the two leaks
  Each check ran in the same folder, launched exactly as the implementer would
  be. Kept in `runs-2/`, they show:
  - the hidden folders as empty stand-ins, including the other run's folder and
  its `.tmp`;
  - no network (403);
  - no writing outside the folder;
  - Bash as the only tool.

  **The two leaks:**
  - **Each run saw the other's folder name** (`fpk-py`, `fpk-go`). That could
  tell it that a reader in another language existed, but nothing of its content.
  I searched both readers' code, tests, questions and summaries for `fpk-go`,
  `fpk-py`, `sibling`, `blind` and `other reader`. The only hit was the Python
  run's own temporary folder.
  - **A Provenance sentence in section 3.5**, "Both blind readers stream the
  text and write it", could have suggested a choice. Python made that choice,
  and Go made the opposite one. Neither cites the sentence: Go argues from
  section 6's bound, Python from the brief's two answers.

  Neither leak shows in what the runs made. Naming the folders alike would close
  the first. The second is the specification's to close.

  ### A third reading from the record alone
  The README's "Running a third reading from this record" gives the exact steps:
  - dependencies and corpora;
  - bringing `samples.ts` up to a changed format first;
  - making the samples and the module's answers;
  - `prepare` and its two-line check;

  ## Third fork: the facts
  A probe went out press by press from reading size on the five-file picture.

  | Press | Scale | Look | Names cut | By, on screen |
  | ---: | ---: | --- | ---: | --- |
  | 0–2 | 1–0.694 | reading | 0 | |
  | 3 | 0.579 | connections | 6 | 13, 10, 3, 5, 7, 16 px |
  | 6 | 0.335 | connections | 6 | 7, 6, 1, 3, 4, 9 px |

  - **What sets the floor:** a constant. `ARRIVAL_FLOOR_RATIO` is the ladder's
  sixth press, 1.2⁻⁶. It measures nothing of the picture.
  - **The cut is the look's, not the floor's.** The same six names are cut at
  every press of the connections look, by the same amount in map units (about
  22, 17, 5, 9, 12, 27). The floor is where it is smallest on screen.
  - **Why:** in that look a name is one line drawn at the held scale 2.2.
  `committedLiveDocGraph` is 121 px wide at its font, so 266 drawn, in a label
  244 wide. It clips across with no ellipsis, no second line and no title.
  - **Where the room goes wrong:** the card reading's pass reckons the room
  across as 277, 288 and 314 for three of these rows; their labels as drawn are
  244, 234 and 266. Names between the two widths are the clipped ones. This is
  read from the numbers and the code; I did not step through the pass.
  - **The design system:** visual foundations, principle 5: "Where room runs
  out, the name is cut with an ellipsis or leaves the picture; it is never drawn
  smaller." So a cut with an ellipsis is allowed; these six have none, and that
  rule refuses them too. On these names the two instruments agree. They differ
  only on a name cut with its ellipsis: the rule and the zoom looks' cases
  accept it, the still picture's count refuses it.
  - **Proposed mend, not built:** the look gives the name its room. Make the
  card reading's room across for the connections look the label's width as drawn
  there; too-wide names then take the second line their slot already has, and
  names too long for two are cut at a seam with ellipsis and title, as the pass
  already does elsewhere.
  - **Why not the floor:** a floor by measure would not help. Every press of the
  look cuts the same names, so it would land on the reading look's last press
  and the arrival would hardly pull back.
  - **Picture:** `repo--pointer--3-released.png`. `symbolMatchesAnchor` on two
  lines is the mended form; `MAX_ENUMERATED_…` is the cut form.
  - No instrument was relaxed. The three still-picture cases were not rerun this
  milestone and nothing was changed that would turn them green.

  ## Mirror, third milestone: the turn is built and holds a person's place with
  a held card, but it stutters on the heavy subject

  One commit, `391b4764b`, on `worktree-agent-aa5d0c3fbbddda338`, on top of
  `46e13bf60` (fast-forward merge, no conflicts). This round took about 105
  minutes, much of it waiting in the lane (one wait of 422 s) and on builds
  under load.

  ### What is built
  - **The lever.** Tuning has a Mirror Turn select in the 2D layout group (Turn,
  Slide, Cut), wired as Way Down is, so a link can carry a non-default choice.
  **Turn is the default in this trial**, so the owner meets their own idea
  first. The lever count is 82.
  - **Turn.** The plane turns 0 to 180 degrees to mirror and on to 360 to come
  back, so the right side goes away from the viewer in both directions. The axis
  is through the held card in your order, else the middle of the view. Every
  card and directory line turns back by the same angle, so no name is seen from
  behind. Pins and badges change sides at the edge-on instant.
  - **Slide.** The same machinery with no viewer's distance, the angle chosen so
  each card's place moves in a straight eased line.
  - **Cut.** A fade out and in, a third of 620 ms each way. Reduced motion
  always takes it.
  - **Reuse.** I read the three perspective modules first. They cannot serve:
  that move copies the picture, folds the copies to tokens and moves them
  between two drawings on a canvas, with no turning plane, and making its copies
  alone outlasts this motion. Only `transitionEase` is shared. The stylesheet
  draws the turn from one angle written on the map's layer each frame; nothing
  is copied and no wire is rewritten until rest.
  - **New files.** `mirror-turn.ts` (pure), `mirror-motion.ts` (the page's half)
  and `mirror-turn.test.ts`. They are separate modules, so
  `reading-direction.ts` is untouched by this round.

  ### A fork I decided: the fade at edge-on
  The first strip showed the card swinging nearest the viewer, grown by its
  nearness, covering the subject whole at the edge-on instant. Everything but
  the held card, wires included, now fades to 0.12 at edge-on and back. The held
  card is never boosted.

  ### Strips and my eye
  Thirteen strips are in `Screenshots/mirror/` with the README: seven frames
  each at 10, 25, 40, 50, 60, 75 and 90 %. I looked at five; eight were taken
  and not looked at, including the Cut strip.

  - **Turn, estate hub, held card:** the hub never moves or dims. Neighbours
  swing, the near ones crossing it as faint ghosts. At edge-on it stands alone
  with pins already exchanged. A person keeps their place. For about the middle
  third nothing else can be read.
  - **Turn, heavy config, held card:** right frame by frame; the subject holds
  throughout.
  - **Turn, doc-tools, nothing held:** the directory's membrane shows as a
  tilted sheet at 25 % and 75 %, which says the picture is turning. At edge-on
  almost nothing is on screen. Nobody keeps a place because there is none; it
  reads as a fade with a hint of travel, no better than the cut. The owner's
  "probably look like trash" is half right.
  - **Slide, pathfind:** cards pass through one another as a faint pile. The
  subject holds. It explains less than the turn.
  - **Wires, every form:** they read only in the first and last quarter, because
  the cards face the viewer while the wires' plane turns. Redrawing wires per
  frame was not tried.

  ### Cost on the heavy subject
  `liveDocumentationConfig.ts`, 84 cards and 218 wires, headless with the page
  composited on the processor, lane held alone. Each cell is frames, total ms,
  longest frame ms.

  | Form | Speed | To mirrored | Back |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | Turn | full | 5, 1,217, 500 | 6, 1,467, 500 |
  | Slide | full | 6, 1,500, 450 | 6, 1,317, 467 |
  | Cut | full | 25, 1,017, 350 | 27, 733, 183 |
  | Turn | slowed 4× | 4, 5,950, 3,200 | 5, 5,300, 2,500 |
  | Slide | slowed 4× | 4, 3,183, 2,417 | 5, 4,950, 2,567 |
  | Cut | slowed 4× | 22, 3,300, 2,183 | 21, 2,883, 1,867 |

  **Turn and Slide stutter here even at full speed**: a 620 ms motion drew five
  or six frames, so a person would see three or four jumps. The cut's long frame
  is the rest itself (pins restyled, picture measured, 218 wires redrawn),
  which every form pays once. I did not take apart where the turn's remaining
  time goes, and did not measure a light picture or a browser compositing on a
  card.

  ### Tests
  - **`mirror-turn.test.ts`, five cases:** written before the functions and seen
  failing on a module without them. They were not seen failing against a broken
  formula.
  - **The second-press case:** seen failing for a real reason. Two presses left
  the camera 1,547 px off. Coming to rest redraws the wires, the page hears

  first one's rest; the press then started another over it. Mended, and it

  - **The lever case and the reduced-motion case:** pass, but were **not seen

  - **The lever counts:** seen failing at 81 in both unit cases before the bump
  to 82.
  - **Final build:** all eight cases of `local-map-mirror.spec.ts` pass through
  the lane.
  - **Other checks:** lint, 1,391 unit cases (`packages/explorer`,
  `tests/e2e/scenarios`), the links check and the three audits pass. The audits
  ran before the last mend, which touched two source files only.
  - **`tuning.spec.ts`:** on the build before the mend, the lever count and the
  rest passed. "marbles on and off each compile" hit its 30 s timeout under
  load; it is a 3D case, and I did not re-run it alone.

  ### Left for you

  card is held; nothing recommends it over Cut where none is.
  - Whether the turn should fall back to the cut above some picture size, or its

  ## Milestone 6: the review's three findings mended, the left-out measure
  taken, the graph's keys mended; the left-out rule not yet built

  Branch `worktree-agent-ac72d8a99b54c2e5d`. I merged `bcd44843b`, then the
  reviewer's branch (`0d780aacf`, through merge `10fc51368`, with no conflicts),
  and added:
  - `c6d0435e9`: key the graph's files in a table that holds their paths alone,
  so a file named `__proto__` or `constructor` is a file like any other.
  - `a4493e8ce`: mend the review's three findings in this road's code, and
  measure what kept files import from code a scan leaves out.
  - `4c349e643`: record the page check green in all 51 folders.

  ### The review's three findings (done first)
  - **1. The manifest reader now follows Node's documented resolution step for
  step** (`PACKAGE_EXPORTS_RESOLVE` and `PACKAGE_TARGET_RESOLVE`):
    - `exports` is read only when it is neither `null` nor `undefined`.
    - A `null`, an empty list or a list ending in `null` under a matching
  condition ends that side, with nothing exported.
    - A target is refused unless it is `./` followed by segments none of which
  is empty, `.`, `..` or `node_modules`.
    - In a list of fall-backs, a refused item is passed over, and the list
  answers with its last item's answer.
    - A manifest that mixes `.` keys with condition keys, or uses an array index
  as a key, is refused.

    The reviewer's seven cases are green, and its shapes have joined the made
  shapes in `json.exports.test.ts` (18 cases). The oracle over the installed
  packages still agrees on 279 of 281.
  - **2. The repeated `go.mod` asks are gone.** `moduleOf` now asks the listing
  what lies at each folder's `go.mod` before reading it, and the reviewer's case
  is green. I found the same probe-written-as-a-read in the JSON reader's
  read-ahead of `tsconfig.json`, and it now asks the listing first too.
  - **3. The page check counts failed reads.** The page's own host (`tree.read`)
  now counts every ask, found or not. The `node:fs` stand-in still counts only
  reads that find their file, because its asks are for the docs written (the
  sink's).
    - **Red** on `go/depot` from a copy of the code before the Go mend: 32 asks
  for eight absent `go.mod` files (37 files asked 61 times in all), with
  `report/` and `stock/` asked 8 times each, as the review counted.
    - **Green** on the mended code: all 51 folders pass with every ask counted
  and the differences ledger empty (20 fixtures in 2.9 min, 31 programs in 2.1
  min, one hold each). The Go depot's 29 files are now asked 29 times.
    - I ran the red case from a copy of the worktree, so the lane wait (about 5
  min behind the arrival and mirror holds) did not block the mends.

  ### Item 1: code a scan leaves out that a kept file imports
  `left-out-imports.mts` runs the engine's own `collectDependencies` and
  re-export walk twice on every kept script file: once reading the disk, as now,
  and once asking the listing. The listing run uses a frozen copy of the engine
  with the undone `dependencies.ts` move re-applied by `listed-resolution.py`.
  Nothing is generated, so the read-only corpora were read in place, at a
  machine load of 11 to 16.

  imports of a left-out file, `export *` from one, `export { … }` from one,

  - **"Through `node_modules`" is zero by construction.** The TypeScript

  ## Third leg: A measured, and the choice is not plain; the fifteenth case is
  still red

  The review's fifth case is still the only red one. Only design 1, with a
  file's own lists decoded lazily, can make it green. That changes what a reader
  returns: a read-back `StoredFile` would become a view instead of a plain
  object. I'm asking for a ruling before building it, as you allowed.

  **Commit:** `f24591391` "Measure the two designs that might bound a
  stored-form reader's cost", on `worktree-agent-a5522d16dedb937a3`, on top of
  `a272c52e4`. It holds measures and the record only: `design-a.mjs` and
  `tab-worker.mjs` beside the record, under "Fifth milestone, third leg". No
  module code changed. Lint, `docs:links:enforce` and the audits pass. I did not
  run the unit project again, since only the record and probes changed.

  ### What the consumers ask, from the page's code
  - **The map** (the world map's model and controller): every file's paths,
  wires or edges, archetype and entries.
  - **The search** (`graph-search.ts`, `search-field.ts`): every file's symbol
  names.
  - **The reader's pane** (the detail panel, `sources-facts.ts`): one file's
  whole facts.
  - **The command line:** one file's facts, or walks over the wires.

  ### Under Node, fresh process each, load 17–27
  - **Design 1:** a copy of the module whose quick open keeps only the section
  it last decoded. A validation walk checks everything and keeps only the
  strings, paths and wires.
  - **Design 2:** the whole read in a worker.

  | | DNN: time / peak / held after | 5× DNN: time / peak / held after |
  |---|---|---|
  | Today's whole read | 2.1 s / 238 / 87 MB | 4.5 s / 475 / 277 MB |
  | 1: validation walk | 2.4 s / 226 / **47 MB** | 4.3 s / 373 / **76 MB** |
  | 1: one file asked after the walk | 68 ms | 45 ms |
  | 1: search walk over every name | 2.9 s / 217 / 57 MB | 4.3 s / 371 / 74 MB |
  | 2: scan cloned out of the worker | 3.7 s / 324 / 81 MB in caller | **10.8 s
  / 827** / 368 MB in caller |
  | 2: kept in the worker, asked by message | 1.9 s, then 8 ms an ask / 253 / 12
  MB in caller | 3.5 s, then 8 ms / 507 / 20 MB in caller |
  | 2: graph cloned out of the worker | 4.8 s / 371 / 101 MB in caller | 11.5 s
  / 1,018 / 458 MB in caller |

  ### In a tab, through the lane (three holds of under 2 s each)
  - **Under today's policy a page cannot start a worker.** Chromium refuses the
  blob worker by the page's `script-src` hash ("Creating a worker from
  'blob:null/…' violates … script-src"). Design 2 on the page therefore needs a
  `worker-src` in the policy, which is the owner's to weigh against the single
  hashed script.
  - **Not resolved:** with `worker-src blob:` added, the module worker still
  failed with no message, on DNN and five times DNN. First suspects: a module
  worker from a blob in a page opened from disk, or the worker inheriting the
  page's policy.

  ### What the numbers say
  - **Design 1 cuts what is held after reading,** 3.6 times at five times DNN
  (277 → 76 MB), in about the same time, and answers one file in 45–68 ms. It
  barely lowers the peak while reading.
  - **Design 1 as measured does not bound one section.** A section is 256 files
  and up to 64 MiB of records.
  - **The fifth case needs design 1 carried into a file's own lists.** It asks a
  million symbols' `length` within 54 MB. No form that builds those symbols
  passes: V8's smallest six-field object is about 70 bytes, so a million is
  about 70 MB. Design 2 cannot pass it either, since the case reads in-process.
  - **Design 2 contains the cost; it does not reduce it.**
    - Cloning across the boundary doubles time and memory, so it is not a way to
  hand a page its facts.
    - Kept in the worker and asked by message, each ask is cheap, but the worker
  holds the whole read as today.
    - What it buys is a ceiling: a hostile file ends the worker, not the page.
  - **Recommendation (your guess, now measured):**
    - design 1 for memory: views, plus one validation walk, which turns the
  fifth case green;
    - design 2 as the page's protection, by message only and never by cloning,
  subject to the policy change.
  - **The window (B4)** survives design 1, since its validation walk is still a
  whole read in order. It is to be built after your ruling, at 8 MiB, with its
  case.
  - **The second reading's finding 5** (nothing bounds the graph: a 442-byte
  file gives an edge for each long label) belongs here. It will be named in A's
  specification text when design 1 is written up; not yet done.

  ### Not reached
  - Design 1 built (needs your ruling on the view API).
  - The tab worker under a `worker-src` policy, debugged.
  - The window with its case.
  - C, D, G, and what the page does with bidirectional controls.
  - **The second blind reading's additions**, received during this leg and not

    - the end line's five variants as cases (the first leg's mend should already

  ## Sixth milestone: all five mends are done and committed

  The five are the reviewer's three path faults, the dual-direction wording, and
  the ledger test's exits and streams. Each case was seen red on the unmended
  code before it went green. One page case fails on this branch, and I believe
  it is not caused by these changes (section below).

  ### Commits
  I merged `bcd44843b`, then the reviewer's branch at `0d780aacf`; both
  fast-forwarded. Later I merged it again at `77f47429c`, which made merge
  commit `e37153d35`. None of the merges conflicted.
  - `b60f847a5`: find a path between nested ends, refuse a directory name two
  spellings share, and end a symbol's path to itself (items 1–3).
  - `34cd5ca37`: say only what each walk tested when a search both ways finds
  nothing, and read the ledger's exits and streams (items 4–5).

  ### 1. Nested ends
  **The rule now lives in one place.** The engine's `pathfind.ts` gains
  `endsApart`. The page and the command line both walk through it, because
  `walkBetween` calls it.
  - When one end holds the other, the outer end is taken without the inner one.
  - When the two ends are the same, there is no path.
  - For ends that overlap any other way, the starts are kept and the files they
  share with the targets are dropped from the targets.

  **What it prints**, on a workspace where `src/a.ts` uses `src/b.ts`:
  - `--from src/ --to src/b.ts` prints `Path from src/a.ts to src/b.ts, 1
  hop(s): each file uses the next; asked from src/ without src/b.ts.`
  - `--from src/Pay --to src` prints `…; asked from src/Pay/ to src/ without
  src/Pay/.`
  - The same end on both sides, a directory or a file, prints `No path runs
  between src/ and src/: the two ends are the same.` and exits 1.
  - **The JSON** has `asked: { from, to, narrowed }`, where `narrowed` is
  `from`, `to`, `same` or null.

  **Red before:** the reviewer's 4 cases in `pathfind.overlapping-ends.test.ts`.
  4 of 5 were red with the old engine file; all 5 pass now.

  **Page:** I built the bundles and ran its directory cases through the lane
  (`--grep director` over `local-map-path.spec.ts` and `search-bar.spec.ts`).
  - 4 passed, including `local-map-path.spec.ts:370` (a directory as a path's
  end) and the destination field's directory counts.
  - The page does not yet say that an outer end was taken without the inner.
  That text is the page builder's.

  ### 2. A directory named in another case
  - One directory answers: it is taken and named back as spelled. `src/PAY` in a
  workspace with only `src/Pay` gives `asked.from: "src/Pay/"`.
  - Two or more answer: the command refuses with exit 1, `src/PAY names 2
  directories that differ only in case: src/Pay, src/pay; give one as it is
  spelled.`
  - Files already behaved this way.
  - **Red before:** the reviewer's two-directory case in
  `inspect.case-folded-directory.test.ts`.

  ### 3. A symbol's path to itself
  - It now needs at least one hop, so `src/a.ts#a` to `src/a.ts#a`, or to
  `src/a.ts`, prints `No symbol path found …` and exits 1.
  - **Red before:** the reviewer's symbol case.

  ### 4. Searching both ways
  **Text:**
  - A way that finds nothing says only what that walk tested.
    - Forward: `<from> does not use <to>` (for a directory, "No file of X uses a
  file of Y"), directly or through other files.
    - Reverse: the same sentence with the ends swapped. Estate: `No file of
  Contracts/ uses a file of PaymentService/, directly or through other files.`
  - Ends are named by their paths as asked, never by their last segment.
  - Found steps read `uses` and `is used by`.

  **JSON:** the payload is now `{ kind: "dual-direction", asked, forward,
  reverse }`, and each found way has `each`, `from`, `to` (the files that
  answered) and `nodes`.
  - One-way paths carry the same `asked`. Their first line names a directory end
  as asked: estate `--from Portal/Pages/Default.aspx --to PaymentService` gives
  `…: each file uses the next; asked to PaymentService/.`
  - **Removed:** the top-level `from` and `to` of `kind: "dual-direction"`,
  which now live under `asked`. No test or consumer read them.

  **Red before:** the reviewer's `inspect.reverse-path-wording.test.ts`, and my
  case for the reverse wording and asked ends. My one-way `asked` case already
  passed, since that field came with items 1–3.

  ### 5. The ledger test's exits and streams
  - An equivalent must exit 0, and its fragments are read from standard output.
  - A gap's refusal must exit 1, and its symptom is read from standard error.
  - A row can name its own `exit` and `stream`. Only "a path at a moment" does,
  because its gap shows as an answer on standard output with exit 0.
  - **Seen failing each way:** I temporarily gave one yes row `stream: stderr`,
  one gap row `exit: 0` and one gap row `stream: stdout`. Exactly those 3
  failed. Put back, all 33 pass.
  - The parity document says how exits and streams are read.

  ### The ledger's totals
  Unchanged: 32 rows; 22 with an equivalent (4 of them only as files or JSON); 9
  gaps (7 with no command, 2 where a command lacks part); 1 the command line's
  own. The directory row's expected text gained `; asked to PaymentService/.`

  ### Forks

  - **The page's held first line now gets a clause.** It gains a trailing clause
  (`; asked …`), but only when an end is a directory; between two files the
  line is exactly as before. Tell the page builder.
  - **Item 2's wording** is the reviewer's proposal: refuse, naming each
  spelling.
  - **The refusal's tail:** refusals go through `UsageError`, so the
  two-spellings message ends with the standard `Run "npm run live-docs:inspect

  **Left to you:**

  ## Handback: the C# documentation-reference oracle now compares declarations,
  and the reader's overload faults are mended

  Branch `worktree-agent-aa78a3cf3e0d25678`. It was reset to `769b51894` and the
  reviewer's branch merged in as `88966b412` (no conflicts). Two commits on
  top:

  - `97d8e1a1f`: the oracle compares each documentation reference with the
  compiler's declaration by declaration. Committed, with its register, before
  any reader change.
  - `7d6244b64`: the reader tells overloads apart and links nothing where none
  matches. It also carries the dated record section and the corpus measurements.

  ### The oracle (commit 1)
  - **The compiler program** (`csharp-compiler.cs`) now prints three more things
  for each `cref`:
    - `on`: the line of the declaration its comment documents.
    - `declarations`: the file and line of each declaration the cref names (each
  part of a partial type).
    - `holder`: for a member, the type that holds it.
  - **Unparsed references.** A `cref` Roslyn's grammar does not take is not a
  cref node to Roslyn but an ordinary attribute. It is now kept with outcome
  `unparsed`. On DNN there are 396 of them, the `M:…` documentation IDs; the
  reviewer's `L::Calc` is another.
  - **The comparison.** `compareDocumentation` matches the reader's reference to
  the compiler's by file, the documented declaration and the name written.
  Same-file references are included.
  - **Causes.** `notRead`, `holder`, `unresolved`, `more`, `fewer`, `elsewhere`,
  `unparsed`, `unwritten`, `transitive`, `framework`, `compilerNone`.
    - `framework` now applies only where a parameter is a framework keyword or a
  type no file declares. The oracle reads that syntax itself
  (`writesUndeclaredParameter`), not through the reader.
    - Matching uses the reader's own name normalization, `crefLinkTarget`,
  exported from `csharp.xmldoc.ts`. It only names references, so both sides are
  keyed alike.
  - **Ambiguous references.** A method named with no parameter list where
  several overloads stand (CS0419) still names every candidate, and a reader
  that links them all agrees, as the record had decided.
  - **New sample file.**
  `tests/integration/programs/csharp/names/App/Overloads.cs` (code makes no edge
  to another file), with an authored Purpose and Notes in its Live Doc. Its
  overloads differ only by:
    - an array mark or its rank
    - `ref` or `out`
    - generic type arguments
    - `params`

    It also has a one-parameter constructor, four references no overload
  answers, and `M::Mixer`, which the compiler cannot parse.
  - **Sample records.** All five were written again. Edges, names and the
  earlier `cref` fields are unchanged; only fields were added.
  - **The register.** The `DOCUMENTATION` register in `csharp-oracle.test.ts`
  now holds an `agree` count and every disagreement by cause, so it fails when a
  count moves. The stand-in test in `csharp-compare.test.ts` covers every
  cause.

  ### The oracle's tables
  | | before | after |
  |---|---|---|
  | names sample (30 refs) | 17 agree; 8 `more`, 4 `compilerNone`, 1
  `unresolved` | 30 agree |
  | reviewer's projects (22 refs, extended) | 9 agree; 8 `more`, 4
  `compilerNone`, 1 `unresolved` | 22 agree |
  | DNN (8,032 refs compared) | 6,883 agree; `notRead` 616, `holder` 84,
  `unwritten` 310, `unresolved` 43, `framework` 36, `elsewhere` 27, `transitive`
  26, `compilerNone` 7 | 6,889 agree; `unresolved` 37, the rest the same |
  | mojoPortal (35 refs) | 18 agree; `holder` 15, `notRead` 2 | the same |
  | estate, rosetta, basic, webforms | 1, 1, 0, 0 agree, no disagreements | the
  same |

  File pairs are unchanged: DNN 1,312 compiler, 1,313 reader, 1,259 both;
  mojoPortal 4/4/4. In the before run, mojoPortal's three unparsed references
  were counted among the ambiguous ones; I fixed that count before the after
  run.

  The reviewer's two classes do not occur on either corpus. The six DNN
  references that now agree are all `params` overloads: five in `DnnLogger.cs`
  (`DebugFormat(IFormatProvider,string,object[])` and its kin) and one
  `inheritdoc` in `StepBase.cs`. The per-reference lists are committed as
  `authors-word/declarations-dnn.txt` and `declarations-mojoportal.txt`.

  ### The reader (commit 2)
  - **`parameterShape` keeps what tells overloads apart:**
    - the passing modifier (`ref`, `out`, `in`, `ref readonly`)
    - the type's simple name with its type arguments, read recursively
    - array marks and rank, pointer marks and `?`
    - It ignores `params`, `this` and `scoped`.
    - A `?` on a type not known to be a value counts only when nothing matches
  with it: on a reference type it is just an annotation, and the reader cannot
  tell a struct.
  - **`params` parameters are counted.** The grammar splits `params T[] x` into
  three children of the list, so those overloads were one parameter short. A new
  `declaredParameters` reads them and keeps every parameter's modifiers.
  - **No match, no link.** There is no fallback to overloads with the same
  count, and none to the type for a constructor reference that no constructor
  answers. The name stays as text ("a suggestion is never a fact").
    - When a type's own overloads don't match, the ones it inherits are
  searched; constructors are not inherited.
    - `Type()` on a type that declares no constructor names the type, where C#'s
  implicit constructor is.
  - **Nested generics render correctly.** `normalizeCrefTarget` turned only the
  first pair of braces into angle brackets (`Box<Box{Mixer>}`); now all are
  turned.

  ### Cases and failing first
  - The register at `97d8e1a1f` held the 13 wrong answers before the mend. That
  committed register is the case that failed first, and I saw the same wrong
  answers on the reviewer's projects.
  - The reviewer's `csharp.cref-overloads.test.ts` is now green (5/5). I did not
  run that file itself before the mend.
  - The stand-in unit test passed on its first run. I did not switch a cause off
  to see it go red.

  - **`elsewhere`, 27 on DNN, new:** `LogException` and

    - 3 are `PropertiesDictionary` (declared once in each branch of an `#if`).

  The whole unit project passes: 3,744 passed, 4 skipped, in 246 files. By your

  **Commit:** `d184837ff` "Read a stored scan as a view, so that reading it
  keeps no file's facts", on `worktree-agent-a5522d16dedb937a3`, on top of
  `f24591391`. The record is under "Fifth milestone, fourth leg" in
  `Probes/stored-form-module/README.md`.

  **What held:**
  - `npm run build`, `npm run lint`, the three audits, `docs:links:enforce`, and
  `live-docs:generate` with its `--check` all pass. The package's suites pass

  on the view's `get` trap.
  - **A first run of the unit project this leg failed 35 cases, all from the
  machine.** The load was 35, with 1,749 processes.
    - 30 were the engine's discovery oracle, each a `git config` in `/tmp`
  failing with no message.
    - 5 were five-second timeouts.
    - Run alone at load 12, the oracle passed 60 of 60. The whole project then
  passed as above.

  ### Design 1, built
  - **The whole read is now a walk.** `readScan` reads every section once, in
  order, with every check. It keeps the string table, the paths, the wires and
  each records section's inflated bytes, and no file's facts; the last decoded
  section is released when the walk ends.
  - **`files` is a view:** a read-only list that decodes each file from its
  section's kept bytes when read and keeps nothing.
    - To any reader it behaves as an array: its length, indexes, iteration,
  `map`, `Array.isArray` and JSON all work.
    - Writing to it is refused.
    - A file read twice may come back as two equal objects rather than one.
  - **The quick open now keeps only the section it last decoded.**
  - **Every other package case passes through the view unchanged**, the fuzzer's
  included.
  - **On the three corpora,** the graph read through the view still prints byte
  for byte as each `index.json`, and both round trips are exact. This repository
  has 1,336 files, the estate 41 and DNN 10,777.
  - **DNN at load 12–23:**

    | Read | Time |
    |---|---|
    | First answer | 162–194 ms |
    | Walk (facts read and checked) | 1.18–1.48 s |
    | Graph as asked | 1.75–2.31 s |
    | Whole graph | 1.88–2.10 s |

    Peak memory was 226–326 MB. The graph as asked now costs about 0.6 s more
  than before, because its index pass reads every file through the view,
  decoding each a second time. Gathering the inbound and documented-by numbers
  during the walk would remove that; not done.
  - **Spec §6 now says what a reader promises,** and how far the cases hold it:
    - after the walk, memory is what the file inflates to;
    - a file's facts are decoded when asked, and what is held is what was asked
  for.
  - **Named as not yet done:** a file's own lists are still decoded whole when
  the file is asked for, so a million symbols' count still builds the million
  symbols. That is the view's next step. §6 also names a graph printed by a
  consumer as not bounded by the file's size (the second reading's fifth
  finding).
  - **The inflation bound stays at 64.**

  ### The tab worker, written into the record exactly, for the page's probe

  ## Seventh milestone handback (06:57 UTC, inside the fifty minutes)

  All three items are built and committed on branch
  `worktree-agent-ac72d8a99b54c2e5d`, on top of `4c349e643` with no merge:
  - `634ca047a`: "Never read what a scan leaves out: resolve a script's imports
  from the run's listing, and look a path up one way in the Explorer"
  - `92bb73043`: records the page check's result.

  The tree is clean and my scratch folder is removed. No command was refused.

  ### 1. What a scan leaves out is never read
  - **Where it reads now.** `packages/engine/src/live-docs/dependencies.ts`
  resolves a relative or alias import only among the files the run's listing
  holds (`pathKind`, which falls back to the disk only when no run is handed).
  `fileExists` and the `node:fs/promises` import are deleted. The re-export walk
  is handed the same reads and listing. The resolvers are now synchronous;
  `resolveDependency` still returns a promise for its callers.
  `sourceAnalysis.ts` and `discovery.ts` pass `files: fileIndex`.
  - **The Node-needs list.** `tests/page-scan/node-needs.json` loses
  `dependencies.ts`'s `node:fs/promises` `stat`. No reader names `node:fs` now.
  - **Imports of a path the scan keeps no file for.** They keep the label the
  kept file wrote, and a re-export of everything such a path offers names
  nothing. `sourceAnalysis.ts` reports both as notices, so the run's last lines
  count them (files listed under `--verbose`):
    - "imports of a path where the scan keeps no file, left unread and labelled
  as written"
    - "re-exports of everything a path offers where the scan keeps no file,
  their names unknown"

    This repository has neither. A made workspace gave 1 and 2.
  - **The history's case** moved in the same commit, with its reason written
  into it. It is renamed "never reads excluded code an included file imports and
  re-exports, as a checkout does not". The labels are now `../assets/logo.png`,
  `../generated/api`, `../generated/types` and the re-exports are `[]`. The
  other 13 cases pass, so the history still equals a checkout.
  - **Whole comparison of this repository's docs.** 8 docs were updated and 1
  created, and every one is the doc of a file this milestone edited. A second
  generate run changed nothing.
  - **What a doc says.** Not built. The leave-out function is not wired into the
  scan (step 5), so the scan cannot tell a left-out path from one that does not
  exist. A word written beside every unresolved relative import would also
  change the docs of imports of files that are simply missing, which the ruling
  does not move. Building it needs the leave-out function's answer per path,
  handed to readers, plus a round trip through the doc format.
  - **Proposed wording.** It sits after the label, like the existing
  "(type-only)":
    - First choice: `(left out)`. It is short, says what the scan did, and uses
  the leave-out function's own term.
    - Alternative A: `(not scanned)`. It is plainer for a non-software reader,
  but would also read true of files the scan reads and does not document.
    - Alternative B: `(left out by generated/**)`. It names the rule as evidence
  and is the most honest, but uses more of our words and needs the rule handed
  per path.
  - The design doc's step 4 (`.mdmd/layer-3/engine-and-hosts.mdmd.md`) and the
  record (`AI-Agent-Workspace/AgentSessionHistory/2026/11/2026-11-22/Probes/engi
  ne-hosts/README.md`, new dated section "seventh milestone") now match what was
  built.

  ### 2. One way to look a path up, in the Explorer too
  - **The four files.** They now look a path up through `fileAt`:
  `shared/graph.ts` (both edge targets), `client/reader-links.ts`,
  `client/panels/sources-facts.ts` (`archetypeOf` takes `GraphFile | undefined`)
  and `client/views/worldMap/model.ts`. `card-reading.ts` is untouched.
  - **New test.** `packages/explorer/src/client/prototype-names.test.ts`
  generates a workspace with files named `__proto__`, `constructor`, `toString`
  and `hasOwnProperty`. It parses the index as plain objects, the way the page
  does, and puts one case to each of the four lookups. `valueOf` must come back
  as no file.
    - At the old tip, reader-links (reading `find`) and the world map (reading
  `length`) threw on `valueOf`. The other two passed there and are routed
  through `fileAt` all the same.
    - The test first lived in `shared/`. That pulled client code into the
  non-DOM explorer project, broke the build and emitted stray
  `packages/generator/src/generator.{js,d.ts,*.map}`. I moved it to `client/`
  and deleted those four ignored files. Its Live Doc has an authored Purpose and
  Notes.
  - **The command line.** `scripts/live-docs/inspect.ts` gets one assertion
  where the graph enters: it throws if `Object.getPrototypeOf(graph.files) !==
  null`, naming `graphFilesByPath` and `withFilesByPath`.

  ### 3. The duplicate test file

  ### Finding for you
  The TypeScript reader marks "(type-only)" per import statement, not per name.
  So `import { fileAt, type GraphFile }` shows `GraphFile` without
  "(type-only)"; `reader-links.ts`'s doc lost that mark this way. 258 files in
  the repository use this inline form, so the gap is the reader's and is not
  new.

  ## Sixth milestone, first leg: tasks 1–3 built, each case seen failing first;
  the format does not rest yet

  C and D, which change the format, are not built. The third blind reading

  **Commit:** `b8c20fd55` "Decode a stored file's own lists as they are read,
  gather the graph's index in the read, and inflate within a window", on
  `worktree-agent-a5522d16dedb937a3`, on top of `3cd947724` (merged in plain
  first). The record is under "Sixth milestone, first leg" in
  `Probes/stored-form-module/README.md`.

  **What held:**
  - The package's suites: 73 passed, 4 skipped.
  - Lint, `docs:links:enforce`, the three audits, and `live-docs:generate` with
  `--check` are all clean.
  - On this repository (1,349 files), the estate (41) and DNN (10,777), the
  graph read back still prints byte for byte as each `index.json`, and both
  round trips are exact.
  - **The whole unit project, run once: 3,776 passed, 4 skipped, 1 failed.** The
  failure is the history's identity case "names as roots every package…", a
  five-second timeout.
    - Run alone at a load of 41, it took 8.6 s.
    - It reads only the engine's and generator's sources, which this work does
  not touch.
    - It is the same load-bound case recorded in the second milestone (8.2 s
  alone at a load of 15).
    - On a quieter machine the whole project should pass; I could not get one
  this leg.

  ### 1. A file's own lists, decoded as they are read
  - After the walk, a file's entries, symbols and dependencies are lazy lists.
  They go through one codec method, `items`.
  - Decoding a file walks each item once to learn where it begins, keeping four
  bytes per item and nothing else. An item is decoded from its start only when
  it is read.
  - **Case:** a file of 300,000 symbols, read back.
    - Asking its symbols' count must keep under 4 MiB, and symbol 123,456 must
  read back as it was written.
    - It failed against the module before the change, holding 63,037,408 bytes.
  It passes now.
  - **What §6 now says, without the old caveat:** a count keeps four bytes a
  symbol, not the symbols. Finding where the lists end still walks each item
  once, transiently, keeping nothing.
    - So "builds no symbol" is true of what is kept, not of every allocation.
    - A skip that allocates nothing would need a second walk of the grammar
  inside the module.

  ### 2. The graph's index gathered during the walk
  - **What changed:** the walk now gathers documented-by as file numbers,
  alongside the wires it already checks. Inbound comes from the wires section.
    - A scan read back carries both, kept apart in a `WeakMap` keyed by its file
  view.
    - `graphViewOf` uses them instead of decoding every file a second time.
  - **A fault mended on the way:** `graphViewOf` kept every file it decoded in a
  map by path, which undid the view. It now looks files up by number.
  - **Measured at a load of about 7, interleaved**, for the graph as asked (the
  hub's Local Map):

  | | Before | After | Peak memory |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | DNN | 1.86–2.55 s | **1.04–1.32 s** | 360 → 234 MB |
  | Five times DNN | 5.39–6.94 s | **1.72–2.08 s** | 433–535 → 311 MB |

  The graph as asked now costs about 30 ms beyond the walk itself.

  ### 3. The window at 8 MiB, with its case
  - **What changed:** `READ_AHEAD` is 8 MiB.
    - The read inflates the strings and records sections in order, and ahead of
  the one being checked only within that constant.
    - Only sections whose headers passed every header-only check are inflated
  ahead.
    - So a file refused by one section has cost at most 8 MiB of inflation past
  it.
  - **Case:** 24 incompressible records sections of 2 MiB each, after valid
  paths, wires and strings, with the first section refused at its first byte.
  Memory held beyond the file, sampled every millisecond during the read, must
  stay under `READ_AHEAD` plus 8 MiB.
    - With the window made unbounded it failed, holding 120.5 MiB. At 8 MiB it
  passes.

  ### Not reached
  1. C and D (format changes).
  2. The thirty-five questions answered in the text. None are answered yet, so
  there are no per-question answers to report.
  3. G.
  4. The page and bidirectional controls.

  No command was refused. The scratch folder is removed.

  ## Engine review, fourth pass: nothing here should stop the history rebuild or
  the landing on `main`

  **Nothing I found should stop the history rebuild or the landing on `main`.**
  The one finding is a sentence the command line prints to deny a path between
  nested ends, and it is false as worded. It changes no fact in the graph, the
  history or the page.

  The pass is committed as **`3798683d3`** on
  `worktree-agent-a33555ec369858a9e`, on top of `7449cfea8`. The record
  continues under "2026-11-22, at the close: the fourth pass" in `AI-Agent-Works
  pace/AgentSessionHistory/2026/11/2026-11-22/Probes/engine-review/README.md`.

  No guarantee broke twice by different means.

  ### Finding

  **A wrong statement: between nested ends, the denial of a path is worded from
  the ends as typed.**
  - **Input:** `src/lone/w.ts` uses `src/lone/z.ts`, and `src/a.ts` stands
  beside them.
  - **First command:** `inspect --from src/lone --to src --direction both`. It
  says `src/ taken without src/lone/` once at the top, then prints `No file of
  src/lone/ uses a file of src/, directly or through other files.` and `No file
  of src/ uses a file of src/lone/, …`.
  - **Second command:** `inspect --from src --to src/lone --direction outbound`
  prints `No path runs from src/ to src/lone/ (outbound), nor the other way.`
  and says nothing of the narrowing.
  - **Why it is wrong:** `w.ts` is a file of both ends and uses `z.ts`, so each
  sentence is false as printed. `emitDualDirectionResult` and the one-way
  "nothing found" answer word their denials from the typed ends, not from the
  ends `endsApart` actually walked.
  - **Where it came from:** two of the day's mends from different branches meet
  here: the second pass's sentence for the search both ways, and the rule of
  nested ends.
  - **What is fine:** the default answer carries the narrowing clause and is
  true.
  - **Test:** `scripts/live-docs/inspect.narrowed-end-denials.test.ts`, red. It
  first confirms the fact it relies on through the command line's own neighbours
  of `w.ts`.

  ### Two decisions for you (no tests added)
  - **Which direction each surface prefers when paths run both ways.** The page
  and the command line agree on whether a path exists in every case below. When
  both directions have a path, the command line tells the one along what
  `--from` uses, and the page's `findPath` tells the one along what uses
  `--from`. For example, `--from src/a.ts --to src`: the command line answers
  `a.ts uses b.ts`, the page answers `c.ts uses a.ts`. Both are true. The
  question is whether one question should get one answer on both surfaces.
  - **The scan root as an end.** The engine's `under` and the page take `""` as
  every file, and the page finds a path from the root to `src/b.ts`. The command
  line refuses: `--from .` gives "Unable to resolve --from '.'", and `--from
  ""` says `--from` is required. Either accept the root or refuse it plainly.

  ### Red by my files
  One case is red: `inspect.narrowed-end-denials.test.ts`. `npm run lint`
  printed nothing but its usual multiple-projects warning; I read its output
  through a pipe, which hides the exit status. `npm run build`, the three
  slopcop audits and `docs:links:enforce` exit 0.

  ### What held
  - **The path rule, text against JSON, and the command line against the page's
  `findPath` through the same function.** Text and JSON agree and both surfaces
  find a path in exactly the same cases:
    - nesting three levels deep, both ways (`src`, `src/sub`, `src/sub/deep`):
  each path runs between the inner end and the rest of the outer, and the answer
  says which end was narrowed;
    - a directory whose only file is the other end, both ways: "the two ends are
  the same", exit 1, `narrowed: "same"`;
    - a file and its own directory, both ways;
    - one directory in two spellings (`src` and `src/`, `src` and `SRC`): "the
  same";
    - `--direction both` between nested ends: the paths are right, and only the
  denials are wrong (the finding above).
  - **A path at a moment of the history:** already registered in the parity
  ledger as a gap ("gives the links between the two, not a path") and held by
  its symptom; not re-measured.
  - **The registers.** Each check would catch a made change, but I found this by
  reading the checks, not by mutating and running them:
    - the PowerShell hand reading: differences, arguable rows and unscored rows
  by exact equality, with the agreeing count derived from them;
    - the SQL oracle: `disagreements` equal to `MEASURED_MISSES` for each
  fixture;
    - the page check's `differences.json`: both unlisted and gone entries are
  reported;
    - the package register: its counts are compared whole.
  - **Stated bounds.** On a file using 205 others and used by 250, the
  neighbours list 200 each way with `… and N more, not listed`. The JSON gives
  `count` 205 and 250, 200 files each, and `cut` 5 and 50. `--reachable` and
  `--groups` claim no bound and list everything.
  - **Cases the merges may have made vacuous.** The path finder's cases in the

  ### C: a link and what it names, held once
  - **What changed:**
    - The layout and the text no longer hold a target or an anchor. `linked` is
  the link alone, and the marks' bits 1 and 2 are now undefined and refused.
    - What a dependency's, a type's or a documentation reference's link names is
  derived from the link, its document's path and the head's location, by the
  link rule. The rule is now `linkNames` in the module.
    - Wires, the edges' `to` and `toSymbol`, and the documentation relation all
  come from the rule.
    - A reader caches the rule by document folder and link (by the document
  itself for a fragment-only link).
  - **The rule is written out in full as spec §4.3.1, step by step:**
    1. the fragment as written, from the first `#` up to a second;
    2. the path before the `#` and before the `?`;
    3. percent-decoding, keeping the path as written when an escape is malformed
  or the bytes are not UTF-8;
    4. a path beginning `letters://` names nothing;
    5. `.` and `..` folded against the document's folder;
    6. nothing above the scanned folder;
    7. the docs' prefix;
    8. the extension, compared in Unicode's default lower case.

    §4.5, §5 and §8 now refer to it, and §12's "link rule" open question is
  marked resolved.
  - **The sink:** the probe's `scan-of-graph.ts` now hands links over as
  written.
  - **The tests:**
    - The made scans no longer carry targets.
    - The seeded scan's links are now relative to its one-folder-deep files
  (`../<path>.mdmd.md`), so most land.
    - The review's test file still passes a `target` in some cases. The writer
  reads only what the model holds, so those cases run untouched and pass.
  - **On the three corpora,** the graph derived by the module's rule prints byte
  for byte as the engine's `index.json`, and both round trips are exact. File
  sizes:

  | Corpus | Before | After |
  |---|---|---|
  | This repository (1,349 files) | 1,137,321 B | 1,121,919 B (−1.4 %) |

         -rs" | "both changed";

         +s not kept" | "both changed, one taken";                       
      63 
      64  /**
      65 - * `kept`: the merge holds the one side's change, or nothing ch
         -anged. `lost ours` and `lost theirs`: one side changed         
      66 - * the part and the merge holds the other's unchanged copy. `bo
         -th changed`: both sides changed it and the merge holds         
      67 - * exactly one of them, so the other's words may be gone; a mer
         -ge that holds neither is taken as joined by hand.              
      65 + * `kept`: the merge holds, whole, the part of the one side tha
         +t changed it. `ours not kept` and `theirs not kept`: one       
      66 + * side changed the part and the merge holds something else, th
         +e other's unchanged copy or a piece. `both changed, one        
      67 + * taken`: both sides changed it differently and the merge hold
         +s exactly one of them, so the other's words may be gone.       
      68 + * Where both changed it and the merge holds neither, it was jo
         +ined by hand, which no comparison can judge: `kept`.           
      69   */
      70  export function authoredVerdict(parts: { base: string; ours: st
          ring; theirs: string; merged: string }): AuthoredVerdict {
      71    const { base, ours, theirs, merged } = parts;
      71 -  if (ours === theirs) return merged === ours || merged !== bas
         -e ? "kept" : "lost ours";                                      
      72 -  if (ours === base) return merged === theirs ? "kept" : merged
         - === ours ? "lost theirs" : "kept";                            
      73 -  if (theirs === base) return merged === ours ? "kept" : merged
         - === theirs ? "lost ours" : "kept";                            
      74 -  return merged === ours || merged === theirs ? "both changed" 
         -: "kept";                                                      
      72 +  if (ours !== base && theirs !== base && ours !== theirs) retu
         +rn merged === ours || merged === theirs ? "both changed, one ta
         +ken" : "kept";

      74 +  if (merged === (oursUnchanged ? theirs : ours)) return "kept"

      75 +  return oursUnchanged ? "theirs not kept" : "ours not kept";  
      76  }

  The first half is done and ready to merge: the instrument's reading now costs

  **Commits** (branch `worktree-agent-ae93ec956c2910cd3`, on base `3cd947724`,
  tree clean)
  - `587d9867b` is the work-in-progress commit you asked for first.
  - `f707d8cbc` finishes it: the numbers at two loads, the spec runs, the lanes
  comment, the authored Live Doc notes.

  **Your guess is true, but it is the smaller part**
  - Under the browser's profiler on the lanes picture (516 wires, 113 cards,
  247,314 samples; load 7), 69 % of the reading is `getPointAtLength`. Chromium
  walks a path from its start at every call, so a wire sampled every 8 px costs
  about its length squared.
  - Each sample asked against every directory, lane and card is 18 %, plus 5 %
  in the fill test it reaches.

  **The mend in `tests/e2e/still-picture.ts`**
  - **Fewer questions:** a wire's samples are taken first, and only boxes that
  meet the samples' box are asked. Each card's box is read once and cards are
  looked up by file.
  - **A shorter walk:** each segment of a wire gets a short path that stands in
  for everything before it as one straight line of the same length, as the
  browser itself measures it. Of 40,961 samples on 108 wires, none differed in
  any digit.
  - **Fallback:** a wire whose short paths do not measure the wire's own length
  exactly is walked whole as before. So is a stub, and any path not written in
  absolute M, L, C, Q.

  **Numbers, with the machine's load**

  | Picture | Run (1-min load) | Reading as it was | Reading now |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- |

● I think your feeling is right, and the record backs it more than I expected.
  Nothing here needs an answer from you; the trial will put the open choices in
  front of your eye.

  Why they are the same idea

  Both toggles ask one question: what decides where things stand, what they use
  or where they live?

  ┌─────┬─────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────┐
  │     │       By connections        │            By directory             │
  ├─────┼─────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┤
  │     │ Connections: references     │ Structure: files hang from their    │
  │ 3D  │ pull the layout and are     │ folders; references hidden at rest, │
  │     │ drawn                       │  shown on demand                    │
  ├─────┼─────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┤
  │     │ Flow: files ordered by who  │ Grid: cards by folder, no line      │
  │ 2D  │ uses whom, one line per     │ until you pin a file                │
  │     │ pair                        │                                     │
  └─────┴─────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────┘

  The 3D Structure rule is your own, from October 14: code connections in the
  tree are "definitely hidden at rest. Revealed on demand". The old 2D grid
  behaves the same way under a different name.

  The "draw connector lines" tangle

  It comes apart with one rule that both maps already follow: the picture draws,
  at rest, the relationship that arranges it. The other kind waits for a
  pointer, a pin or a path. So the lines are not a second switch; they follow
  from the first.

  Where 2D really differs

  In 2D, position carries direction: what offers stands left of what uses it.
  Lines laid over cards ordered by folder would run backward about as often as
  forward, which you have refused since last December. 3D has no such axis, so
  it can reveal a connection over the tree. This is why 2D by directory needs
  your cue rather than faint lines.

  What this changes in the trial

  - One control on both maps. I'll build the 2D choice as the twin of the 3D
    one: same words, same place in the toolbar. In 2D by directory, the
    connections side lights and counts what the arrangement is not drawing.
  - I take back my objection to amber. Amber already means "connections this
    picture cannot draw in order", shown on the control that leads to a picture
    that can. That is what your cue would say.

  Choices the trial will show

  - Whether the choice is remembered once for both maps or separately. Today 3D
    opens by connections, and the calm 2D you describe opens by directory.
  - What pinning a file does in 2D by directory.
  - The words: Connections and Structure, or By Connections and By Directory.

  The landing

  The mirror's turn mend is reviewed and good. I sent one thing back: the
  default stays the Turn you played with, not the plain fade an earlier rule of
  mine would have shipped. That and the arrival's last round are due shortly,
  and the history rebuild is half done.

  ## The open point: six whole names the audit still counts
  On the five-file Local Map the still picture reports the six names cut off by
  9, 7, 6, 4, 3 and 1 px. These are the amounts they ran past their buttons
  before any mend, at this scale. The names are whole and inside their buttons'
  boxes. `design-audit.ts` has `const own = contentBox(node)`, so text in the
  button's padding counts as outside it. I changed nothing for this.

  Decision for you:
  1. **The audit says which check found each cut, and the still picture sets
  aside only an own-width finding on a row name the standard calls whole (my
  recommendation).** Clips and sibling overlaps on those names stay faults, and
  the content-box rule stays everywhere else. I recommend it because a button
  whose padding is its own name's room is not text crowding a padding.
  2. **A reach that is content.** A negative margin alone takes the reach off
  the row's contribution. A `min-width` to give it back looks possible but is
  intricate where a name is wider than its button at reading size. It would need
  the card measure again.

  It holds the law's plain sight, not the rule.

  wire, in the wires' own SVG, with `pointer-events: stroke`, then checks the
  hit test is handed that stroke where the card stands, and then both readings
  must see the card.

  **Fork decided:** the case makes its own stroke and does not give the page's
  band its pointer back. That way it does not depend on the page having bands at
  all, which is the independence the rule is for. If you would rather it use
  the page's band, it is a few lines.

  I read the case's two pictures again with each card's rows taken three ways:
  in order, as a set (name, height, pinned by name), and its ports as a set.

  | | Rows in the layout's order (default) | Rows in order of appearance |
  | --- | ---: | ---: |
  | Cards | 79 | 79 |
  | Same cards, same wires | yes | yes |
  | Cards standing elsewhere in the second picture | 48 | 46 |
  | Cards of another size | 0 | 0 |
  | Cards with other rows | 0 | 0 |
  | Cards with other ports | 0 | 0 |
  | Cards pinned otherwise | 0 | 0 |
  | Cards whose rows stand in another order | 1, the subject | 0 |

  - **Your first rule holds.** Which rows a card shows, which are pinned and
  which carry a port do not depend on where the pin was made. The 78 differing
  lines are 26 rows of the subject's card in another order, each with its two
  ports.
  - **The order follows the cards' places, by the product's own rule.** Symbol
  Order's default is the layout's; its help says it "Orders a card's symbols by
  where their wires lead". `orderRows` in `branch-order.ts` keys each row by the
  mean place of the cards its wires lead to.
  - **The cards do stand elsewhere.** `still-picture.spec.ts`, which the four
  rows at the head of the difference all lead to, stands at y 10,016 in the
  first picture and 9,295 in the second. `local-map-path.spec.ts` stands at
  11,420 and 11,219.
  - **The control run isolates it.** With rows in order of appearance, which the
  layout does not move, no card's rows differ although 46 cards still stand
  elsewhere.
  - **Why it showed now.** The case holds each row's place inside its card,
  which holds the order. I take it the two orders simply agreed until this
  repository's own `still-picture.ts` changed today; I did not check an earlier
  commit to confirm that.

  ## The fork
  Whether a reader should get one order of a card's rows however they came:

  1. **The order is the picture's, like the cards' places (my recommendation).**
  The case would hold what the claim is: the same cards, sizes, rows, pins,
  ports and wires, but not the order of rows inside a card. It already does not
  hold where the cards stand. Nothing in the product changes. This is the
  reading that matches what the product says of itself.
  2. **One order however the reader came.** The order would be chosen again when
  the rows are laid out at reading size. That is the continuing search, which
  this spec turns off. I could not measure whether it brings the two pictures to
  one order: with the search on, the map did not reach rest within the
  instrument's 30 s wait, three runs of three on this 79-card picture. It would
  also move cards under a reader who has just come back.
  3. **A subject that does not change (the estate).** It would stop this
  repository's own edits from moving the case, but the same disagreement can
  arise there, so it does not settle the question.

  If you rule the first, the change is one comparison in
  `overview-files.spec.ts` and a sentence beside the one about the cards'
  places; I have not made it.
